### W3C DID Resolution
<a id="w3c-did-resolution"></a>

**W3C, specification.** Candidate Recommendation Snapshot 2026-08-06; the latest Candidate Recommendation Draft is of 2026-11-03 (publication history on api.w3.org, read 2026-11-09, under the shortname did-resolution-1.0), after drafts of 2026-10-24, 2026-10-25, 2026-10-26 and 2026-10-30. The draft of 2026-10-01 (also 2026-08-28) marked the Decentralized Identifier (DID) URL dereferencing feature and sections 5, 6, 8 and 10 as at risk; the at-risk markings of the later drafts were not read. The exclusion opportunity on the Patent Review Draft ended on 2026-10-05. PR #344, "full pass at DID URL dereferencing algorithm (isolated)", was merged into main on 2026-10-13 at 02:10 UTC (merge commit 0956c93). The W3C publication history read on 2026-10-13 still lists the Candidate Recommendation Draft of 1 October 2026 as the latest, so no draft has been published after the merge; the merged text was not read for the at-risk markings. At the DID Working Group (WG) call of 2026-10-08 (minutes at w3.org/2026/10/08-did-minutes.html, read 2026-10-13) a chair ran the poll "PROPOSAL: Merge PR344 and agree that any future improvements to the DID URL Dereferencing Algorithm should be handled as separate issues.": eight participants recorded +1 and one recorded -1, "some technical issues not sufficiently discussed, charter violation, process violation, breaks compatibilty with DID 1.0 and CID". The chair then said "by Chair's decision, we will be merging this PR" and asked that the technical points be raised as separate issues; the merge followed on 2026-10-13. The minutes do not record the poll as a resolution. The PR comments of 2026-10-08 record the same dissent, that the changes violate the WG charter, and the chairs' answer that they do not. The statement of 2026-10-06 that unresolved points of the PR would be run as proposals so that objections can be recorded was followed by this single poll; separate issues for the points of the -1 were not looked for one by one on 2026-10-13. (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** The specification defines how a DID is resolved to a DID document and how DID URLs are dereferenced; the relativeRef parameter is used for product-level references such as Digital Product Passports (issue #332). PR #344 (merged 2026-10-13) was described on 2026-10-02 as replacing the dereferencing algorithm with handlers and dropping built-in processing of relativeRef and serviceType (the latter from GS1); the merged text (38 commits, rebased on 2026-10-11) was not compared with that description; a participant wrote (https://github.com/w3c/did-resolution/pull/344#issuecomment-5611077919) "This breaks various existing deployments and use cases that I have explained on WG calls, e.g. in the area of Digital Product Passports." On 2026-10-05 a chair (Will Abramson) wrote that "the chairs plan to run a proposal to gauge the consensus of the group for merging this PR", and that otherwise "maybe the group has to start seriously considering ripping out dereferencing entirely from the specification." He approved the PR the same day ("Needs a rebase, but other than that looks good to me"). Exclusion opportunity closed 2026-10-05; no patent disclosures or exclusions listed on the DID WG intellectual property rights (IPR) page as of 2026-10-06. On 2026-10-07 a chair (Will Abramson) wrote on PR #344 "The chairs intend to run a proposal to adopt this PR and replace the existing DID URL Dereferencing section."; another participant approved the PR the same day, noting a concern about the MUST on giving the user the choice of resolvers, and a third participant wrote "I support the idea that we run a proposal to merge this PR on Thursday." On 2026-10-08 a participant wrote on the PR "In my opinion, the changes in this PR violate the WG charter" (and, he adds, like the previous "Resolve DID URLs" proposal) (comment 6060762625); the same chair answered the same day "In the chairs view, as has been stated numerous times on the calls, their is no charter violation." (comment 6062369708). On 2026-10-08 the same participant wrote that "The overloading of service type in this algorithm in my opinion requires a Class 4 change in DID 1.1, which is not allowed by the" (comment 6064294182). The same day that chair wrote for the chairs "My sense is the group is faced with two options", the first to "Adopt this PR as the new foundation for the DID URL Dereferencing algorithm" and the second to "Remove DID URL Dereferencing from this version of the specification" (comment 6062369708). In a review comment the chair wrote "This diagram needs to be updated to 2. Resolve the DID" (4220468912) and a participant answered "Ok. I can get that in today." (4221259495); commit 697f09b of 2026-10-08 by that participant, is titled "removed DID URL from dereferencing diagram". Merge: the PR was merged on 2026-10-13, 02:10:47 UTC (GitHub merge record, read 2026-10-14). PR #344 has no comment after 2026-10-08 (read 2026-10-13); the public-did-wg archive for October 2026 held three messages, the last of 2026-10-07, and the outcome of the call of 2026-10-08 is in its minutes (see the status).

**Committed next.**
- None declared in the sources read.

**Boundary.** WG resolved on 2026-09-10 that the specification "will not define an abstract Dereference() function signature" separate from Resolve().

**Author participation.** The author of this map took part in PR #344 with two comments on 2026-10-02 (relativeRef handler, serviceType); see Disclosure.

### W3C TPAC 2026 breakouts on agents (Dublin, 2026-10-26/30)
<a id="w3c-tpac-2026"></a>

**W3C, event.** Technical Plenary and Advisory Committee (TPAC) 2026 took place from 2026-10-26 to 2026-10-30 in Dublin (hybrid). On 2026-11-02 breakout #25 (permissions, actions and alignment) is marked "Canceled" on the TPAC calendar, and its issue was closed on 2026-10-28 "by request of the proponent"; for #22, #34, #69, #71 and #84 the calendar showed the session as past and "Confirmed", a scheduling status. Read 2026-11-09: draft minutes are published on w3.org for #22 (2026-10-30, an IRC log with no resolutions; the co-chairs also posted, linked from the issue on 2026-11-02, a summary "reconstructed from the Zoom recording and transcript", "a summary, not verbatim"), #34 (2026-10-28), #69 (2026-10-26), #71 (2026-10-28) and #84 (2026-10-28, an IRC log without a scribe), so this map records these five sessions as held. Breakout #95, on identity and payments of agents, was not held: its issue was closed on 2026-10-26 and the proposer wrote on 2026-10-31 of a slot at a W3C meeting in March 2027. The deadline for breakout proposals to be scheduled in advance was 2026-10-08 and has passed; the README (read 2026-10-13) says that "Around 19 October" the schedule of the breakout sessions for Monday to Wednesday is added to the TPAC calendar, and the calendar page of breakout sessions listed no session in its text on 2026-10-13. On 2026-10-14 (09:35 UTC) that page lists 85 sessions, all marked "Tentative", each with a day, a time shown in Greenwich Mean Time and a room. (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** Breakouts on agents carry the label agentic web in w3c/tpac2026-breakouts; on 2026-10-13 twelve are open and none is closed or withdrawn: #22 The Agentic Web, #23 AI-friendly webpages, #24 declarative and imperative solutions, #25 permissions, actions and alignment, #31 the future of agentic web experiences, #34 Zurich workshop report, #36 extending WebMCP, #39 KYAPay, #56 agents and developer experience, #69 action semantics for agents, #71 user consent when browsers act, #84 WebMCP in WebExtensions. Two proposals without that label matter to this map: #37 a pre-kickoff meeting for the Decentralized Identifier (DID) Methods Working Group (WG) (opened 2026-09-30) and #17 the proposed Digital Policy Working Group charter. On 2026-10-13 the issues carried no slot (labels session and agentic web only); on 2026-10-23 each carries its slot in its body, as a day and a time with no time zone: #69 action semantics for agents, Monday 17:00 to 18:00; #25 permissions, actions and alignment, Wednesday 10:30 to 11:30; #84 WebMCP in WebExtensions, Wednesday 14:00 to 15:00; #34 Zurich workshop report, Wednesday 15:15 to 16:15; #71 user consent when browsers act, Wednesday 16:30 to 17:30; #22 The Agentic Web, Friday 08:30 to 09:30. On the calendar page of 2026-10-14 (09:35 UTC) these proposals appear with provisional slots, matched by title, times as listed in Greenwich Mean Time: #39 KYAPay on 26 Oct 17:00 to 18:00; #69 action semantics for agents on 26 Oct 17:00 to 18:00; #24 declarative and imperative solutions on 27 Oct 15:30 to 16:30; #17 the Digital Policy Working Group charter on 27 Oct 15:30 to 16:30; #36 extending WebMCP on 28 Oct 09:00 to 10:00; #25 permissions, actions and alignment on 28 Oct 10:30 to 11:30; #23 AI-friendly webpages on 28 Oct 10:30 to 11:30; #84 WebMCP in WebExtensions on 28 Oct 14:00 to 15:00; #34 the report and follow-ups from the E-commerce for Humans and AI Agents workshop on 28 Oct 15:15 to 16:15; #56 agents and developer experience on 28 Oct 15:15 to 16:15; #71 user consent when browsers act on 28 Oct 16:30 to 17:30; #31 the future of agentic web experiences on 29 Oct 17:00 to 18:00; #37 the DID Methods WG pre-kickoff meeting on 29 Oct 17:00 to 18:00; #22 The Agentic Web on 30 Oct 08:30 to 09:30. All are marked "Tentative". Issue #22 (opened 2026-09-16, with #24 and #25 on 2026-09-17, all by marcoscaceres) has the goal "Directions for future work at the W3C around enabling the web to work well with AI agents." On 2026-10-23 it names sarony and yoavweiss as additional chairs, its agenda field reads "_No response_", and a comment of 2026-10-01 by a member of the W3C staff says that it "Would be a good place to discuss the charter" of the Agentic Web Working Group (Strategy issue #576; see w3c-agenticweb-wg). The repository w3c/tpac2026-miniworkshops hosts mini-workshops on AI and Society, Age Assurance, and Data Governance and Ownership, with submissions on product data discoverable by agents (#32) and agentic payments (#25).

**Committed next.**
- None declared in the sources read.

**Boundary.** Breakouts are sessions proposed by participants, not Working Group work: TPAC has "a series of breakout sessions determined by the attendees."

**Author participation.** The author of this map is listed among those present in the minutes of breakout #34 (2026-10-28) and among those "Also seen on Zoom" in the co-chairs' summary of breakout #22 (2026-10-30); see Disclosure.

### W3C/GS1 Workshop on E-commerce for Humans and AI Agents (Zurich, 8-9 Sep 2026)
<a id="w3c-gs1-ecommerce-workshop"></a>

**W3C, event.** Workshop held 2026-09-08/09 at Google Zurich, co-organised by W3C and GS1. A report and follow-up session is proposed for Technical Plenary and Advisory Committee (TPAC) 2026 (breakout #34, open on 2026-10-13 with one comment and no assigned slot). A workshop report: not found in the root of the workshop repository nor in its commits (the last push is of 2026-09-09), read 2026-10-13. (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** The workshop covers merchants' context, legal accountability, protocols, trust and ownership, product data, identity and authorisation, and payment; position statements (issues #10 to #34 and #45, and three PDFs in position_statement/ added by PR #43, merged 2026-07-17: Fabacus, KYA-OS, P&G) are each the statement of its author. On seller authority and product identity, position statement #31 by Sven Böckelmann says "Resolution says where the data is; a signature says who signed it. Neither says who may speak for the product."; #21 (GS1 Digital Link) is related. On agent identity, delegation and consent, statements include #22 (FIDO Alliance Payments Technical Working Group (TWG), which says AP2 and Verifiable Intent are "now under review within the PTWG") #26 (Skyfire) and the KYA-OS position statement (PDF), which says agent authorization should be "a rail-agnostic, cross-protocol grant: bounded by scope, spend, and time, chained to an accountable party" and that "a delegation can scope authority to a schema.org Offer or a GS1 Digital Link-identified product class"; its author proposed this addition in issue #21 of this map's repository and states being the editor of the KYA-OS specification, stewarded by the DIF Trusted AI Agents Working Group, and working at Vouched, which donated KYA-OS to DIF and sells commercial products built on it.

**Committed next.**
- TPAC breakout "Report and follow-ups from the E-commerce for Humans and AI Agents" (#34); no slot assigned in the sources read (undated) [statement] https://github.com/w3c/tpac2026-breakouts/issues/34

**Boundary.** The workshop is an event, not a standards group; no scope exclusion found in the workshop repository, 2026-10-03.

**Author participation.** The author of this map took part in the workshop with position statement #14 (issue of 2026-06-25) and PRs #51 and #73; see Disclosure.

### IETF AI Preferences (aipref WG)
<a id="ietf-aipref"></a>

**IETF, venue.** The vocabulary draft is at -08 (2026-09-13 in the datatracker history) and the attach draft was last seen at -05 (2026-08-19). A virtual interim meeting is announced for 2026-10-21. On 2026-10-13 the datatracker record gives revision 08 of the vocabulary draft (time 2026-09-14 06:18 UTC) and revision 05 of the attach draft (2026-08-19), no later revision; the interim announcement was not rechecked. (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** The Working Group (WG) develops building blocks to express preferences about how content is collected and processed for AI model development, deployment and use, and the draft W3C Agentic Web charter lists it for coordination. For a seller, the vocabulary is the layer in which a publisher states what automated systems may do with its content. The editor copy of the vocabulary draft (main branch of ietf-wg-aipref/drafts, read 2026-10-03) has a section AI Use that reads "where the asset is not directly provided by the user." with a note that issue 249 asks whether referencing an asset by URL counts as direct.

### IETF AUDIT (BoF preparation)
<a id="ietf-audit"></a>

**IETF, charter.** Charter text under discussion on the audit list and in mirjak/audit-bof-preparation; the BoF request lists "User e-commerce use case" in its proposed agenda (file updated 2026-09-18). On 2026-10-05 and 2026-10-06 participants agreed in words on the sentence "VCON is considered a suitable record format and the audit group will work with the vcon group to ensure the specified format addresses the auditing"; pull request #61 (one commit, 009b915) was still open on 2026-10-08 and does not contain that sentence. On 2026-10-23 the datatracker group page of audit shows the state Birds of a Feather (BOF) and no charter; a name search for expected-records finds no draft (not found on the datatracker, 2026-10-23), although a list message of 2026-10-10 refers to draft-ahuja-schrock-audit-expected-records-00. On 2026-10-10 a BoF proponent wrote on the list to a participant who had asked for a presentation slot "I am not the chair of the BoF meeting. The chairs will be selected by the ADs." and that the BoF agenda "will not be based on an open call for presentation". Pull request #61 was still open on 2026-10-23 with its single commit 009b915; pull request #62, "Refine language for clarity in AUDIT charter", was merged on 2026-10-19. Read 2026-11-09: pull request #61 is still open with its single commit 009b915, and the sentence agreed on the list is in no commit; on 2026-10-19 Mirja Kuehlewind entered it on the pull request as a review suggestion, worded "The outputs of the VCON WG include a record format that should be considered suitable for the AUDIT WG and collaboration with the VCON WG ensures that such a record format takes into account the requirements for auditing needs."; two participants supported the suggestion on 2026-11-03 and 2026-11-04. The relationship with VCON is therefore under discussion, not decided, as of 2026-11-06. On 2026-10-23 a BoF proponent opened issue #64 of mirjak/audit-bof-preparation, "Adding an out-of-scope section?", which reads "Discussion is on-going on the list. Currently proposed items would be:" auditing of model training; interactive or real-time auditing of agent behavior; requirements on the auditing system itself; authorization and delegation mechanisms or extensions to OAuth; and how and when to create the records ("audit is focused on collection and correlation"). (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** AUDIT concerns audit information about agents and delegated systems, so it touches commerce wherever an agent acts for a buyer and a seller or payer later needs a record of what happened. The charter scope reads "The scope of such auditing is the observable behavior of agents, including user intent, agent or tool interactions, invoked or declined actions, exchanged data, and" Commerce, seller, merchant: not found in the charter text, 2026-10-02; repository example: "booking a hotel room" (issue #16). The BoF request lists under "Architecture overview und use cases (20 mins)" a "User e-commerce use case" and names no presenter; the architecture draft has no "e-commerce" text, and its use cases include a flight booked through a chain of agents and "Financial Transactions by Agents": "Agents may execute financial operations such as payments or procurement actions on behalf of users". The sentence on VCON was proposed on the list on 2026-10-05 by a BoF proponent, Mirja Kuehlewind ("VCON is considered a suitable record format and the audit group will work with the vcon group to ensure the specified format addresses the auditing") and accepted on 2026-10-06 by a participant ("I'm OK with this wording"); another participant proposed a variant in charter style. On 2026-10-06 Sumit Ahuja wrote that he and Iman Schrock are writing an expected-record draft, "We are aiming for the -00 before the IETF 127 cutoff", which "declares which records each participant in a cross-domain chain is expected to produce, what each establishes, and when an absent one becomes a finding"; Schrock listed six conformance cases for it (declaration integrity, all slots filled, bounded observed absence, incomplete observation, wrong or ineligible record, late record) and the claim boundary "no qualifying record was found in the declared sources by the cutoff". No text of that draft was read; it was announced on the list. Case 4 reads "An unavailable source, incomplete pagination or coverage, unresolved participant set, or open window produces an incomplete audit, never an absence finding." On 2026-10-07 the author of this map wrote a seventh case on the list at Schrock's request, "Wrong recipient or holder": a record produced by the right party but held only by another does not fill the slot, and if the declared holder's source cannot be checked the audit is incomplete; the example is the confirmation that an EU trader owes the consumer on a durable medium (Directive 2011/83/EU, Art. 8(7)), held only by the agent platform. a co-author of the draft replied the same day that the case "catches a distinction the six cases do not state clearly" and asked for it to be written in the same shape; the draft text has not been read. The text of PR #61 adds to deliverable 2: "For interaction records - the expectation is that the group will leverage the VCON format. Extensions to VCON to add data needed to model AI" On 2026-10-07 a participant proposed case 8, "Declined action": "A slot for an action that was refused before it ran is filled by the decider's signed refusal bound to that action."; on 2026-10-08 Iman Schrock replied "the refusal should satisfy the decision slot, not fill the downstream effect slot", and that a verified refusal "can make that effect slot not applicable when the declaration names refusal as a terminal condition." On 2026-10-07 Pavel Izmaylov posted the individual draft draft-izmaylov-agent-permission-receipts: the principal signs a permission with a passkey that carries "limits a machine can check" and conditions, and a verifier states for each recorded action whether it stayed within that permission. Schrock replied on 2026-10-08 comparing it with the individual drafts draft-schrock-ep-authorization-receipts-13 and draft-schrock-ep-bounded-capability-receipts-06 and suggested a comparison in Section 11 of the draft. On 2026-09-30 a participant wrote on the list "Let me know if you want me to share some of our practices on EDR and Gateways during any interim or future meetings." (EDR: endpoint detection and response); on 2026-10-08 the lead editor of the expected-record draft answered "thank you for offering text, and yes please." and the participant wrote that the architecture draft and the charter should "identify the necessary PEPs that should be covered first, rather than jumping into defining the record entries directly" (the message does not expand PEPs; policy enforcement points is the usual expansion). On 2026-10-08 the lead editor of the expected-record draft wrote "Both cases go into the -00." (cases 7 and 8), added that the contributors of the two cases "will be named as contributors for these" (see Disclosure for the credit to the author of this map), and wrote "The draft will be draft-ahuja-schrock-audit-expected-records."; the datatracker page of that name returned 404 and the document search found no draft with "expected-record" in its name on 2026-10-09, so the text is pending and its content was not read. On 2026-10-11 a participant wrote a ninth case, "Refusal with an undetermined cause": "The slot is filled by a record that states whether a refusal came from a check that ran and failed, or from a check that", adding "AAE as published collapses both causes into a single DENY, so this case describes a gap in my own specification as much as anywhere else." Iman Schrock answered the same day (his text read as quoted in the lead editor's reply) that where the declaration requires grounds, "a single DENY cannot tell us whether revocation was established or the status check could not finish", and the lead editor of the expected-record draft wrote the same day "This goes into the -00 as Case 9, just like Iman put it. Lars will be named as a contributor for this case." On 2026-10-12 the same participant wrote that the decision receipt format that he publishes "carries Case 9 as fields". The draft itself is still not found on the datatracker (document search for expected-record and audit-expected, and the page and text URLs of draft-ahuja-schrock-audit-expected-records-00, HTTP 404, 2026-10-13), so cases 7, 8 and 9 are announced for the -00 and not read in it. Adjacent work, read on 2026-10-21: the individual Internet-Draft draft-mih-scitt-agent-action-capsule-05 (Steven Mih, Action State Group; -00 of 2026-06-13, -05 of 2026-09-26; no stream and IESG state "I-D Exists" on the datatracker) defines a SCITT profile for recording what an agent did, with the rule "A conforming producer MUST record a Capsule for every verdict" including refusals and timeouts, and an effect type `send_payment`. Not found in the text read: a reference to AUDIT or vCon, a buyer or seller model, a way for a verifier to learn of a Capsule that was not produced. It names the SCITT list as its venue. An individual draft, draft-gruszka-audit-decision-receipts-05 (Informational, 2026-11-02; revisions -00 to -05 were posted between 2026-11-01 and 2026-11-02 UTC), maps the decision receipt of the open-source proofbundle project (repository under b7n0de, the affiliation its author states) and its outcome receipt onto the Interaction, Action and Authorization Transition Records of the architecture, for a human approval, a declined action and an executed action, and states one requirement, "that an approval is honoured at most once" (text read 2026-11-09). The individual draft draft-birkholz-vcon-ai-agent-extension-00 (2026-11-02 UTC) defines a vCon extension for AI agent conversation records (see `ietf-vcon`).

**Committed next.**
- BoF session at IETF 127: Tuesday 2026-11-17, 09:00 to 10:30 San Francisco time (UTC-8), room Continental 6 (datatracker agenda of the meeting, read 2026-10-26). (2026-11-17) [decision] https://datatracker.ietf.org/meeting/127/agenda.json
- Expected-record draft -00, aimed "before the IETF 127 cutoff" (lead editor Sumit Ahuja, co-author Iman Schrock). (undated) [statement] https://mailarchive.ietf.org/arch/msg/audit/RveBSBTLmpMzpU0BGHRfyVnoIt8/

**Boundary.** Scope excludes "the auditing of the underlying AI model, training data, or inference mechanisms" and new identity primitives (issue #7).

**Author participation.** The author of this map took part in issue #35 of mirjak/draft-audit-architecture and the AUDIT list (2026-09-28), and wrote two messages on the AUDIT list on 2026-10-07 proposing and writing the seventh case; on 2026-11-02 he replied on the AUDIT list to the announcement of draft-gruszka-audit-decision-receipts (by his statement); see Disclosure.

### IETF DAWN, Discovery of Agents With Names (working group)
<a id="ietf-dawn"></a>

**IETF, charter.** The IESG approved the DAWN charter on 2026-10-22 and the Working Group (WG) is active: the datatracker group history of that day records "Charter approved, group active", and the group page lists the approved charter as charter-ietf-dawn-01, posted on 2026-10-22 after charter revisions 00-11 and 00-12 (read 2026-10-23). Chairs: Dapeng Liu and Frank Brockners; technical advisor: Kehan Yao; Area Director: Éric Vyncke (Internet Area). The group history records "Chair changed to Dapeng Liu, Frank Brockners from Adrian Farrel, Wes Hardaker": Adrian Farrel and Wes Hardaker chaired the group while it was a BoF and a proposed WG, and were named as chairs of the proposed WG in the WG Review announcement of 2026-10-09. The approved charter says: "The DAWN working group will coordinate with WIMSE and OAUTH WGs as well as other WGs related to DNS (e.g., DNSSD), discovery, identities, or AI (e.g., AGENTPROTO). It will also coordinate with external standards bodies as necessary." Before the approval: the group changed from BoF to Proposed on 2026-08-24; charter 00-09 was on the IESG ballot in early October; charter 00-10 was in external review from 2026-10-09, with comments to the IESG due by 2026-10-19, and was on the agenda of the IESG telechat of 2026-10-22; on 2026-10-22 the "Approve" ballot was closed and the charter state changed to Approved. (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** DAWN is chartered to standardise how a client discovers "public properties of AI resources", so it is the IETF venue where a seller's agent surface would be found. The charter gives three deployment contexts with one example each (approved charter charter-ietf-dawn-01, read on the datatracker on 2026-10-23; the same examples as in charter 00-10): for the local network "within the local network, e.g., discover all agents in this shop;", and for the public Internet "between cooperating parties with trust relationships over the public Internet, e.g., discover all agents of example.org, for all its customers/members.". The WG "will also specify the minimum public discovery information (e.g., type, reachability information, communication protocol, services)."; commerce as a scope item: not found in the approved charter charter-ietf-dawn-01, read 2026-10-23 (commerce, merchant, transaction, payment and purchase do not occur; "shop" occurs once, in the local network example "within the local network, e.g., discover all agents in this shop;"), nor in charter 00-10 (2026-10-13) and charter 00-08 (2026-10-03). On 2026-10-05 the INT Area Director wrote, on a request for a non-WG mailing list for DNS records under underscore labels that carry keys, key commitments or references to them, "While a mailing list will be created, I would like to push back on its name as it misses link to DNS"; the requester proposed the name dns-underscore-keys. An individual survey, draft-jimenez-dawn-discovery-landscape-00 (2026-07-03), compares discovery mechanisms from the IETF, AAIF, 3GPP and open-source projects and lists eight gaps, among them "Well-Known Namespace Pressure"; a text search of it finds no occurrence of AI Catalog, Agentic Resource Discovery (ARD), UCP or commerce (2026-10-06). The individual draft draft-zhang-dawn-agent-discovery-framework proposed a two-layer federated architecture in revision -07 (2026-10-15, text read 2026-10-16); revisions -08 (2026-10-19) and -09 (2026-10-21) followed, and the text of -09 was not read (datatracker, 2026-10-23). The individual draft draft-jakab-dawn-agent-discovery-mdns (revision -00 of 2026-07-06; revision -01 of 2026-10-28, datatracker, read 2026-11-02) describes how Multicast DNS (mDNS) and DNS-Based Service Discovery (DNS-SD) can be used to advertise and discover agents on a local link; it is an individual Internet-Draft, not a document of the WG, and one of its two authors, Frank Brockners, has been a chair of DAWN since 2026-10-22. A second individual draft, draft-moussa-dawn-gap-analysis-01 (Moussa, Akhavain, 2026-06-12), is a gap analysis and applicability statement that maps existing discovery mechanisms to the DAWN problem statement and requirements; a text search of it finds no occurrence of AI Catalog, ARD, UCP or commerce (2026-10-06). The individual draft draft-kay-dawn-use-cases-01 (2026-09-10) names Agentic Resource Discovery as a possible interface to a provider's catalogue in a broadcast use case and adds: "ARD is an illustrative option; protocol choice remains open." The IETF Chair announced on 2026-10-02 that an INT Area Director resigned from the IESG as of 2026-10-01 and that the other co-AD for the INT area is the primary point of contact for the area; the datatracker lists that Area Director as responsible Area Director (AD) for DAWN (2026-10-03). On 2026-10-07 an IESG member entered a Yes with comments on 00-09 that asked the charter to say that the protocol specifications normatively define the minimum discovery information, that the WG is "expected to converge on one mechanism per deployment context", and that "discovering where a catalog is lives in DAWN, and what a catalog contains doesn't"; the member also asked whether DAWN defines how agents are named, and that DNSSD, DNSOP and AgentProto be named in the coordination text. The approved charter names DNSSD and AGENTPROTO in its coordination text and does not name DNSOP; the two quoted sentences are not in it (read 2026-10-23). On the list thread "DAWN Coordination: Discovery Information and Trust Artifact Pointers" Girish Konda wrote on 2026-10-07 that a reference that does not resolve, one blocked by the discovering entity's own retrieval policy, and one that resolves with the record absent are three causes: "All three end as no discovery result, and they have different owners and different retries." On 2026-10-12 a participant who states that they are a co-author of three individual drafts in this space wrote to the IESG on the DAWN list in reply to the WG Review thread "I support chartering DAWN as written in charter-ietf-dawn-00-10", and gave as a reason for keeping identity management of agents out of the first phase "Discovery has to work before anyone argues about who the discovered thing is". Individual drafts read on 2026-11-02 (datatracker and text): draft-king-dawn-requirements-02 (2026-10-19) defines requirements for DAWN; draft-artamokhov-dawn-marketplace-discovery-01 (2026-10-30, intended Standards Track) defines a JSON discovery document at the well-known URI /.well-known/agent-marketplace that points to a capability index's signed catalogue, and its implementation status section lists a reference implementation on six indexes "operated by the author or affiliated parties" and further implementations by the same author; draft-artamokhov-dawn-index-federation-01 (2026-10-30, Experimental) describes how such indexes discover and admit each other and says "No implementation of this document by another party is known."; draft-dinuzzo-best-protocol-02 (2026-10-30, Informational) has services describe their commands and events through a manifest at "/.well-known/best". None of them is a WG document, and none is counted among the discovery paths on the home page. Later revisions on the datatracker (read 2026-11-09; texts not read): marketplace-discovery -02 and -03 (2026-11-02), best-protocol -03 (2026-11-02); index-federation is still at -01. Further individual drafts on the DAWN agenda for IETF 127 (agenda revision -07, 2026-11-06): draft-farrel-dawn-terminology-07 (2026-11-02), "terminology for Discovery of Agents With Names (DAWN)"; draft-cui-dawn-mdi-model-02 (2026-11-02, after -01 of 2026-11-01); draft-seethiraju-dawn-dan-01 (2026-10-30), DNS resource records AIDISCA and AIINDEX for agent discovery; and the three ARD drafts of 2026-10-30 (see `ard`). Working group documents: none on 2026-10-23, none on 2026-11-02, none on 2026-11-09 (datatracker). The datatracker lists no draft-ietf-dawn document; every draft named in this entry is an individual Internet-Draft with no stream, and none has been adopted by the WG.

**Boundary.** The approved charter says "DAWN focuses on this initial discovery only and does not include open-ended search, semantic matchmaking, ranking, or selection of an AI resource based on capabilities or user intent." (charter-ietf-dawn-01, read 2026-11-09). Out of scope in the initial phase, approved charter charter-ietf-dawn-01 (read on the datatracker on 2026-10-23): catalogs of capabilities, all communication beyond the initial discovery process, "AI resource indexing and searching across the wider Internet", "Identity management of AI agents, tools, and skills." and trust management beyond secure content exchange between DAWN parties. In scope as a discoverable AI resource: "a service such as a catalog that will further the discovery process".

**Author participation.** The author of this map wrote in the DAWN list thread "DAWN Coordination: Discovery Information and Trust Artifact Pointers", the latest messages on 2026-09-29, on 2026-10-08 (replying to Girish Konda) and on 2026-10-10 (accepting Konda's wording on absence and incomplete results); the reply of Konda adds a third case to the author's second requirement; see Disclosure.

### IETF Web Bot Auth (webbotauth WG)
<a id="ietf-webbotauth"></a>

**IETF, venue.** Active Working Group (WG), listed for coordination by the draft W3C Agentic Web charter; the first WG draft, draft-ietf-webbotauth-httpsig-protocol-00, was adopted on 2026-09-01, and a two-hour session was requested for IETF 127 on 2026-09-15. Implementation reports and proposed edits to the draft were on the list between 2026-10-04 and 2026-10-13. On 2026-10-13 the datatracker listed draft-ietf-webbotauth-httpsig-protocol-00 (2026-09-01) as the only WG draft, and it still did on 2026-10-26 (API: one draft in the group, revision 00). In the thread on updates to the crawler best practices draft a participant posted a revised text proposal on 2026-10-12 for a distinct Signature-Agent URL, key and key directory per crawler purpose, with pull requests in the WG draft repository and as PR #23 of garyillyes/cbcp. On 2026-10-22 PR #23 ("Identify crawler purposes with distinct Signature-Agent URLs") was merged and issue #3 of the same repository was closed as a duplicate; PR #140 of the WG draft repository, "Separate crawler and agent signing identities", which calls itself a companion to cbcp PR #23, was merged the same day; and revision -01 of the individual draft draft-illyes-webbotauth-cbcp was published (no stream, "I-D Exists", expires 2027-04-25). Its section 2.2 recommends "a distinct Signature-Agent URL, and therefore a distinct key directory, for each crawler purpose"; the text of -01 contains neither "429" nor "Retry-After", and PR #22 of garyillyes/cbcp, on those signals, was open on 2026-10-23. Also on 2026-10-22 the individual draft draft-kelley-webbotauth-sail-00, "SAIL: Source Address Identity List", was published: "a metadata format served at a well-known URI in which the operator of automated HTTP clients publishes the IP prefixes its services send from". On 2026-10-23 the datatracker recorded the webbotauth session at IETF 127 as "Waiting for Scheduling"; on 2026-10-26 the agenda of the meeting schedules it on Tuesday 2026-11-17, 16:30 to 18:30 San Francisco time (America/Los_Angeles, UTC-8), room Continental 4. (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** The WG standardises cryptographic authentication of automated clients (HTTP message signatures, key directories), which a seller or its content delivery network (CDN) can use to tell which agent is calling. The chairs wrote on 2026-09-01 "Based on the feedback on the mailing list, the WG decided to adopt this draft as a WG document." Participants wrote of merchant-side verifiers: "We operate a production merchant-side verifier for automated commerce traffic (Web Bot Auth, Visa TAP, Google AP2)" (2026-08-03) and "We have implemented and use WBA for bot auth at Shopify in production, and we landed WBA interop for UCP" (2026-08-19). On 2026-10-04 a participant from Auth Your Agent reported an implementation in which a user withdraws an agent by serving its key directory with no keys, cut max-age from 3600 to 300 because "an hour is a long time when the agent has just bought something the user disputes", and asked that verifiers respecting Cache-Control become a normative SHOULD in section 5.5.2 (the draft's appendix C.4 says they "should respect Cache-Control"); Thibault Meunier replied on 2026-10-07 "It seems that the simpler solution would be to prevent the agent from making request in the first place." On 2026-10-08 a participant from Akamai, writing as a verifier, supported moving the C.4 guidance into section 5.5.2 as normative text and proposed "Verifiers SHOULD NOT treat a cached directory as fresh beyond its freshness lifetime as defined in Section 4.2 of [HTTP-CACHE], except as permitted by Section", adding that "short signature expires values (Section 6.3) act faster than any directory TTL". The same day a participant, who publishes per-agent keys as a DNSSEC-signed record set and deactivates an agent by removing its key, wrote that "Section 5.5.2 bounds the wrong thing": where the key set reaches a verifier as a signed artifact (section 5.5.3), "the bound is the Appendix B signature's expires rather than any cache lifetime", and he measured on his apex "TTL 300 seconds against a signature window of 14 days and 1 hour". He proposed a sentence for 5.5.2 on the signature's expires and noted that section 6.3 (removal as the only remedy) and appendix C.12 (manual key rotation as the only revocation mechanism, listed as an anti-pattern) read as a contradiction when taken together. that participant answered on 2026-10-08 that his directories sent max-age=300 while the Appendix B proof on each was valid for 7 days, that he had cut the proof to one hour with a re-signature every 15 minutes, and that he supported the text of the participant who publishes per-agent keys. On 2026-10-09 the same participant wrote that "The revocation thread got me reading the rest of the draft" and proposed for Appendix C.1 to replace "or the key is unknown" with "or the resolved directory does not provide the key", because a verifier could read an unknown key as "the key isn't on my list of bots" and so put a new agent that signs correctly in the same "unverified" outcome as a failed fetch; he also asked whether the draft should say anything about an intermediary that signs all its users' requests with one operator key, where removal under section 6.3 withdraws every user at once. On 2026-10-07 a participant asked whether two bots with different purposes from one operator should have distinct Signature-Agent values and proposed a statement "So it's clear different Signature-Agents need to be used for different purposes / use cases."; he wrote that section 4.1 makes the Signature-Agent URL the identifier. A participant from hCaptcha answered that it is similar to an open proposal, pull request #23 of garyillyes/cbcp, and that some crawl operators already do it, and wrote "I would support this proposal." On 2026-10-08 the same participant from Akamai supported a Signature-Agent per crawler purpose on the cbcp thread, citing that section 6.5 of the protocol draft "already says implementations SHOULD NOT reuse a signing key for different purposes and should use distinct keys and directories" and that "A signature that spans several purposes verifies fine but cannot carry that policy."; he asked that the 6.5 example be broadened to "an operator that runs both crawlers and user-directed agents". The crawler best practices draft (draft-illyes-webbotauth-cbcp, individual) was changed on 2026-10-05 (commit cc9942d of garyillyes/cbcp) and announced on the list the same day: a note that the practices "are intended for crawlers" and are "not necessarily appropriate for all automated clients", particularly "user-directed AI agents"; the requirement levels of the recommended practices changed from MUST to SHOULD; the proposal for a central crawler registry removed; and a Security Considerations section written, which says the opt-outs "are voluntary and do not constitute access control or content security measures" and that the User-Agent "is unauthenticated and trivially spoofed". Pull request #22 of garyillyes/cbcp (the author of this map, opened 2026-09-26, 429 and Retry-After in the back-out logic and a 429 on robots.txt read as unreachable) and #23 (opened 2026-10-06 by a participant, distinct subdomains for different crawl types) were both open on 2026-10-09; the only comment on #22 is of 2026-10-06 and reads "Common Crawl has always treated 429 as unreachable.", and in the announcement Gary Illyes wrote "I'm reviewing the pending PR to explicitly cover HTTP 429 and Retry-After". On 2026-10-05 a participant reported that his REDbot implementation emits signatures only when challenged ("the decision to only emit WBA when challenged") and that his agent-census log analyser sees "a reasonable number of bots emitting WBA by default"; he wrote that this is "an interoperability issue" worth highlighting in the document. In that participant's repository mnot/agent-census, pull request #143, "A client with a confirmed Web Bot Auth signature is not a browser", was merged on 2026-11-01 (read 2026-11-09). Thibault Meunier proposed on 2026-10-07 for section 5.3 "An Origin that expects a signature SHOULD request one from clients that did not provide it." in place of "An Origin MAY choose to request signatures from clients that did not initially provide them.", and a paragraph for appendix C, "Signing conditionally", saying that signing only when an origin asks "adds a round trip, and non-idempotent requests may not be safely retried". On 2026-10-08 a participant answered with text under which an origin that refuses unsigned requests "SHOULD include the Accept-Signature field", and "Origins that do not send Accept-Signature in either of these cases SHOULD NOT rely on the presence of signatures in requests unless there is a"

### IETF agentproto (Agent Communication Protocols WG)
<a id="ietf-agentproto"></a>

**IETF, charter.** The Internet Engineering Steering Group (IESG) announced on 2026-10-08, 16:55 UTC, "A new IETF WG has been formed in the Applications and Real-Time Area" for Agent Communication Protocols (agentproto), chairs Orie Steele and Leslie Daigle, and names the responsible Area Director. The datatracker page read on 2026-10-09 shows charter-ietf-agentproto-01, state "Approved", Working Group (WG) state "Active", area Applications and Real-Time Area (art), last updated 2026-10-08, and its history records "Charter approved, group active" on 2026-10-08. Reading of 2026-10-13: the datatracker charter page is still headed "Agent Communication Protocols (agentproto) Proposed Charter" while its state is Approved and the group is Active (charter-ietf-agentproto-01, last updated 2026-10-08). The related drafts page lists 15 drafts, among them the individual Internet-Drafts draft-agentic-ai-usecases-requirements-03 (posted 2026-10-10) and draft-rosenberg-agentproto-usecases-00 (2026-07-04); the datatracker page of the first, read on 2026-10-16, shows revision -04, last updated 2026-10-15. The chairs wrote on the list on 2026-10-12 that "Our goal for this topic is to identify candidate starting points for the Use Cases and Requirements document.", that the discussion "identified two drafts for consideration" (those two) and that "We have useful contributions toward a working group document, but have not yet established which draft should be its starting point." In the same thread an author of the -03 draft proposed, for a -04, the requirements B1-7 (checks on resumption) and B1-8 ("If any check in B1-7 fails or cannot be completed, the dialog is not resumed and the protocol is required to signal the reason."), and CMN-12 ("Before accepting a change to a dialog, a participant is required to be able to verify that the change is authorized at that time."); on 2026-10-13 the same author agreed to add to Section 4.2 "If the outcome cannot be determined, the operation remains unresolved, and resuming the dialog alone does not justify resending it." (proposed text read as quoted in the chairs' message and in the reply). On 2026-10-12 a chair answered a thread on implementation evidence with "Please refrain from sending messages regarding implementations at this time." A session request for agentproto at IETF 127 exists in the datatracker (requested duration 2:00:00, last modified 2026-10-08); no timeslot assignment for it is found in the agenda schedule of the meeting (API, 2026-10-13). On 2026-10-26 the agenda of IETF 127 on the datatracker schedules the session on Monday 2026-11-16, 14:00 to 16:00 San Francisco time (America/Los_Angeles, UTC-8), room Continental 5. Read 2026-11-09 on the datatracker: draft-agentic-ai-usecases-requirements is at revision -07, published on 2026-11-02 (after -05 of 2026-10-27 and -06 of the same day); on 2026-11-07 the document was moved to the IETF stream in the agentproto group, with the working group state "Candidate for WG Adoption". (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** The charter ("Agent Communication Protocols") defines an "agentic dialog management protocol" of dialog identifiers, lifecycle semantics and propagation of dialog context, with bindings over existing IETF protocols; it "neither replaces application-layer agent communication protocols nor defines a new transport protocol". Commerce, payment, merchant and purchase: not found in charter 00-04 (saved 2026-10-03). The text of the charter on the datatracker page is still headed "Agent Communication Protocols (agentproto) Proposed Charter". Commerce, payment, merchant, purchase and seller: not found in the charter text of the page (2026-10-09). The coordination section reads "This working group is expected to closely coordinate with other related IETF working groups on dependencies of the reference architecture and the agentic dialog management", and lists five groups of venues: "Security: Web Authorization Protocol (OAuth), webbotauth, WIMSE - on identity, authorization, and security considerations.", "Transport: WebTransport, MoQ, QUIC, TSVWG, httpbis - on data transport.", "Discovery and Operations: INT area, OPS area - on agent discovery and operational considerations.", "Evidence and transparency: SCITT, RATS, on software and hardware security." and "Conversational data: vCon - on conversation representation and its relationship to dialog context." On the list thread "Topic: Existing practice, use cases, and deployment models" Iman Schrock proposed on 2026-10-05 for section 3.3.5 of the use cases text: "If a required authorization input cannot be established, preserve that fact separately from an evaluated refusal, even when both lead to local denial."; Girish Konda wrote the same day "An unestablished input is a different fact from a negative one" and pointed to his individual draft draft-konda-agentproto-evaluation-state-00. On 2026-10-07 a participant wrote, without asking to change or delay 00-04, that "accurately recording that an authorization answer was unavailable is not the same thing as making that fact available to the decision that admits the continuation." On 2026-10-08 the responsible Area Director answered three participants on the list: "I believe this matter is one for the working group rather than the charter", adding "The use cases and requirements deliverable is the right home for the more detailed discussion of requirements and corresponding text."; the Internet Architecture Board (IAB) reviewer wrote the same day "The new charter looks good to me." Charter PR #128 (Mallory Knodel, opened 2026-10-02) was closed without a merge on 2026-10-08; the responsible Area Director commented "The PR is a substantial change very late in the process, and the concerns it aims to address were already handled by other, more targeted" The milestones of charter 01 read "Dec 2026 - Adopt an informational use cases draft", "Feb 2027 - Adopt an informational architecture draft", "Mar 2027 - Adopt proposed standard agentic dialog management protocol draft(s)" and "Mar 2028 - Submit agentic dialog management protocol draft(s) to the IESG as proposed standard".

### IETF vcon (Virtualized Conversations WG)
<a id="ietf-vcon"></a>

**IETF, charter.** Active working group in the Applications and Real-Time Area, charter-ietf-vcon-01 approved (last updated 2026-03-18); its core draft, draft-ietf-vcon-vcon-core (Standards Track), is at revision -05, published on 2026-10-31 (datatracker, read 2026-11-09; -04 was dated 2026-09-07). The datatracker lists four charter milestones, dated 2023-12-31 (adopt two drafts) and 2024-06-30 (submit them to the Internet Engineering Steering Group (IESG)), and shows no completion state. The virtual interim of 2026-10-08 (14:00 to 16:00 UTC) was held; the editor's message after it is quoted below. Minutes of that interim: not found in the latest 40 messages of the vcon list (to 2026-10-11), read 2026-10-13; the datatracker interim materials were not read. On 2026-10-23 the datatracker still shows draft-ietf-vcon-vcon-core-04 as the current revision and charter-ietf-vcon-01 as approved, and the history of the charter has no entry after 2026-03-18. The virtual interim of 2026-10-22 (interim-2026-vcon-05) was held: its agenda, slides, bluesheets, recording and chat log are on the datatracker, and no minutes were published there on 2026-10-23. Recharter state, 2026-10-13: the datatracker history of the charter shows no revision after charter-ietf-vcon-01 (last action 2026-03-18, a change of Area Director); the recharter text exists only in the repository file changed by PR #37 (merged 2026-10-08) and is not an approved charter. PR #41 on that file had no comments on 2026-10-13; on 2026-10-22, after the interim, it was changed by commit eb8269f, "Remove instructions and approvals, per the 2026-10-22 interim", and it now adds to the AI Agent Extension "references to records in other systems that resulted from the conversation, such as an order or a payment" and the use case "Agent-Mediated Transactions, such as Purchases"; it was merged into main on 2026-10-25 at 03:12 UTC by dgpetrie (merge commit 8e61484), with no comments or reviews, and the merged text is that of commit eb8269f; a later commit on main, 2bcddb9 (2026-10-25, 03:19 UTC, by dgpetrie), removed the limit to a single extension ("Define an extension" became "Define one or more extensions") and fixed a spelling mistake, and left the two phrases above as they were. On 2026-10-25 the datatracker still gives charter-ietf-vcon-01 as the latest revision of the charter (state Approved, new revision of 2023-12-02), its latest event is the change of Area Director of 2026-03-18, and no revision follows -01. Issue #40 of the core draft repository (opened 2026-10-10 by a participant, no comments) quotes section 2.4 of -04, "Objects that refer to a file which is externally stored from the vCon MUST have the parameters: url, content_hash", and says that references to a streaming-service page or to a recording behind a login cannot be hashed; it reports, as its own measurement on a public dataset, that 4,079 of 8,181 containers fail the schema on that one finding (a figure declared by the issue author, not re-run here). (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** The charter defines vCon as a JSON container "to contain and/or reference conversational data, including call style metadata, recordings, data exchanged or presented in the conversations, conversation analysis, transcriptions, translations and annotations", so it is the IETF format for the record of a conversation. On 2026-10-06 a participant proposed on the list to recharter the group to finish the core specification and to add an extension for "ai agent interaction record data"; the charter text he points to adds a baseline format and an "AI Agent Extension" for tool call requests and responses, model details, token usage, prompts and reasoning. The AUDIT effort proposes to treat VCON as its record format for interaction records (see ietf-audit); a related individual draft, draft-rosenberg-cheq-00 (dated 2025-07-24, expiry date 2026-01-25), defines a signed object by which a human confirms an action proposed by an AI agent. On 2026-10-06 the author of this map asked on the list whether the extension's list could name "user instructions and approvals, and references to related records in other protocols"; no reply to that message is among the 6 list messages of 2026-10-06 and 2026-10-07 that were read. On 2026-10-07 the editor of the core draft reported an updated editors copy with "elevation of the party_history events to the top level Events Object" and a Session concept, and that the examples are not yet updated. The editor of the core draft wrote on 2026-10-08, "After discussion in the vCon Interim WG Meeting today, this PR has been merged in to the Charter in the repo."; pull request #37 shows the merge at 15:53 UTC (merge commit 5bd8dbf, file vcon_charter.md in ietf-wg-vcon/draft-ietf-vcon-vcon-core). This is a change to the working group's charter file in its repository; no IESG action on a new charter was found. Pull request #39, a typo fix to the charter, was reported merged on the list on 2026-10-08. The individual draft draft-birkholz-vcon-ai-agent-extension-00 (Standards Track; Birkholz, Petrie, McCarthy-Howe, Rosenberg, Steele; published on the datatracker on 2026-11-02 at 23:29 UTC, 2026-11-03 in Rome) defines the vCon extension `ai_agent` for agent metadata, tool invocations, reasoning traces and system events, and calls itself "a replacement for the former Verifiable Agent Conversation Records specification"; its section 8.5 says that the signed form gives "authenticity and integrity, not non-repudiation", and section 8.6 that "an agent that controls its own recording can omit or falsify entries". The words approval, consent, delegation, mandate, purchase, checkout and commerce do not occur in its text, and payment occurs only in references to PCI DSS (read 2026-11-09).

### Individual IETF drafts on the seller side
<a id="ietf-individual-seller-drafts"></a>

**IETF, work-item.** Three individual Internet-Drafts on the seller side of agent transactions, none adopted by a Working Group (WG): anders merchant-identity-assertions -01, kavian offering-discovery-protocol -01, hood agtp-merchant-identity (-03, dated 30 October 2026, Informational, expires 3 May 2027; -00, -02 and -03 read). The same author's draft-hood-agtp-commerce-01 (30 October 2026, Informational, read 2026-11-02) specifies a message format for agent-to-agent transactions over AGTP and says that it "moves no money, holds no funds, and operates as no payment processor". (as of 2026-11-02).

**Relevance to commerce.** draft-anders-merchant-identity-assertions defines a Merchant Identity Assertion, a signed JSON document binding a legal identity to the merchant receiving a payment, so that an automated client can verify the merchant before completing it. draft-kavian-offering-discovery-protocol lets an agent discover a Service's Collections and Offerings and find links to subsequent operations, for "catalogs ranging from a few Offerings to large marketplaces without imposing a universal product taxonomy". draft-hood-agtp-merchant-identity treats merchant identity as the receiving side of a purchase (-02: "This is the merchant identity gap."); in -03 a merchant is an agent whose Agent Identity Document carries role "merchant", and the abstract says of the asymmetry between the two sides "That asymmetry is the gap this document closes."

**Committed next.**
- None declared in the sources read.

**Boundary.** draft-kavian-offering-discovery-protocol declines a product identifier scheme: its discovery is "without imposing a universal product taxonomy".

### GS1-Conformant Resolver Standard 1.2.1
<a id="gs1-conformant-resolver"></a>

**GS1, specification.** Release 1.2.1 ratified August 2026 (standards page and PDF print no day); the archive page on ref.gs1.org lists "Published 19 August 2026". (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** The standard links an entity identified by a GS1 Digital Link URI, which carries a key such as a GTIN or a Global Location Number (GLN), to multiple sources of related information "in a way that is machine-discoverable and machine-actionable" (section 1), for example product information pages, product data and service APIs (section 2). The query string "does not form part of the entity's identifier", and for a valid URI with no information the resolver returns a simple 404 Not Found (sections 2.12, 2.4.1). On success it "SHALL redirect to the default link unless there is information in the request that can be used to determine a better response" (section 2.6.1); the prose gives no status code for the redirect, and the flowchart in Figure 2-1 shows 307. With `linkType=linkset` or the Accept header `application/linkset+json` it does not redirect and returns the links as an RFC 9264 linkset, and a conformant resolver publishes a Resolver Description File at /.well-known/gs1resolver (sections 2.9, 2.10). The Log of Changes records that an earlier SHOULD to recognise schema.org terms "has been removed although, of course, resolvers MAY use such terms."

**Committed next.**
- None declared in the sources read.

**Boundary.** Out of scope: identical targets or linksets across resolvers, and any particular behaviour for query string parameters.

**Sources.**

- [normative] https://ref.gs1.org/standards/resolver/1.2.1/GS1_Conformant_Resolver_standard_i1.2.1-r-2026-08-07 (2026-11-02)
- [decision] https://ref.gs1.org/standards/resolver/archive (2026-11-09)

### UN Transparency Protocol, Identity Resolver
<a id="untp-identity-resolver"></a>

**UN/CEFACT, specification.** Latest stable version 0.7.0 (GitLab tag of 2026-05-04); the 0.7.0 page says the team "expects to release UNTP version 1.0", without a date. (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** The Resolver Workflow section lists, among "some common conditions that a link resolver service SHOULD manage consistently", "When there is no data for the requested entity ID."; not found in its prose, 2026-10-02: the response for that case. The 0.7.0 OpenAPI artefact defines 404 ("No link resolver entry found for this identifier.") and 400 for a path that does not match the registered scheme patterns. The specification describes the gtr Uniform Resource Name (URN) namespace of the UN global trust register as "IANA registration pending" (see un-gtr-urn).

**Committed next.**
- release of UNTP version 1.0 (no date given) (undated) [statement] https://untp.unece.org/docs/0.7.0/specification/IdentityResolver

**Boundary.** The internal workflow of an identity resolver is not defined; the specification lists conditions a link resolver should handle consistently.

### CEN/CENELEC JTC 24 Digital Product Passport standards (EN 18216 to EN 18246)
<a id="cen-clc-jtc24-dpp"></a>

**Other standards bodies, specification.** Eight horizontal standards; six cited in the Official Journal of the EU according to the CEN-CENELEC page of 2026-07-15 (page re-read on 2026-10-13, unchanged); the two not cited are EN 18239 and EN 18246, and their status is not read. The Official Journal itself was not read: EUR-Lex answered HTTP 202 without content on 2026-10-13. (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** The eight standards cover data exchange protocols (EN 18216), unique identifiers (EN 18219), data carriers (EN 18220), data storage (EN 18221), APIs for lifecycle management and searchability (EN 18222), system interoperability (EN 18223), access rights and security (EN 18239) and data authentication and integrity (EN 18246). Two concern how a product is addressed and found: "unique identifiers" (EN 18219) and "searchability" (EN 18222).

**Committed next.**
- additional standards by product-specific Technical Committees under the 5-year ESPR work plan 2025-2030 (undated) [statement] https://www.cencenelec.eu/news-events/news/2026/en-in-the-spotlight/2026-07-15-dpp/

**Boundary.** Horizontal standards only; product-specific requirements are left to product-specific Technical Committees.

**Sources.**

- [statement] https://ref.gs1.org/docs/2026/GS1-and-SDOs (2026-10-02)

### A2A protocol, agent discovery
<a id="a2a-discovery"></a>

**AAIF, work-item.** A2A registers /.well-known/agent-card.json for a single AgentCard. PR #2240 was merged on 2026-10-21 (23:12 UTC, merge commit 8faec73) into the branch dev-1.1, not into main: at that commit docs/topics/agent-discovery.md calls the well-known URI "A simple option for single-agent deployments." with the limit "One Agent Card per domain.", and heads the next mechanism "AI Catalog Discovery (`/.well-known/ai-catalog.json`)", marked "Preferred" and "the recommended approach for public agents". On main the file does not mention ai-catalog (2026-10-23). Issues #2276 (4 comments, the last of 2026-10-20) and #2248 (no comment), which propose other paths for hosts serving several agents, were open on 2026-10-23. (as of 2026-10-23).

**Relevance to commerce.** PR #2240, merged into the branch dev-1.1 on 2026-10-21, makes /.well-known/ai-catalog.json the "Preferred" discovery mechanism, and issue #2276 proposes /.well-known/agent-card-list.json for providers serving multiple agents. The documentation (docs/topics/agent-discovery.md on main) says "The current A2A specification does not prescribe a standard API for curated registries."; AI Catalog and Agentic Resource Discovery (ARD) not found in that file (2026-10-03); ai-catalog not found in it on 2026-10-23. darrelmiller wrote on issue #1807 (2026-05-12) "Rather than layering trust information into the AgentCard, we are attempting to use AI Catalog to contain that kind of trust information." HTTP Archive custom-metrics PR #176, merged on 2026-10-07, adds a metric for "/.well-known/agent-card.json" to track its presence and key metadata.

**Committed next.**
- None declared in the sources read.

**Boundary.** A maintainer wrote on PR #418 that the A2A working group "will not be building any kind of registry standard".

**Author participation.** The author of this map took part in issue #2276 (two comments, 2026-10-02); see Disclosure.

### AAIF Agentic Commerce Working Group
<a id="aaif-wg-agentic-commerce"></a>

**AAIF, venue.** Charter approved 2026-05-15; Capability Map being built from contributed inputs. Charter targets: Capability Map (2026-09-01) and Recommendations to the Technical Committee on hosted projects (2026-10-01); both dates have passed, and no Capability Map or Recommendations document is found in the repository on 2026-10-13 (root: README.md, charter.md, reporting/; commits to 2026-10-12). Chairs per the August report: Ilya Grigorik (Shopify) and Rahul Bansal (OpenAI). The August report (merged by PR #7 on 2026-10-12) says the x402 session "advanced the payment layer of the Capability Map" and lists no decision needed from the Technical Committee; the report for September is opened as issue #9 (2026-10-12), covering deep dives on onX, MPP and the UCP August release. (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** The group aligns open specifications for commerce workflows carried out by AI agents; the charter says "The Working Group does not author competing commerce protocols" and lists UCP, ACP, Mastercard Verifiable Intent and Visa TAP as "Source Projects (primary inputs to the Capability Map)". The Running Notes record on product discovery (2026-04-01) "Finding products is different from finding where to search". The charter lists "Open Meetings: No". In issue #5 (Capability Map input: cross-boundary evidence and audit row) the author of the individual Internet-Draft draft-krausz-verification-state, who discloses it, wrote on 2026-10-08 that its v_verdict "carries verified, contradicted, indeterminate or not_evaluated" and that "a mapping would need to preserve the distinction between an inconclusive evaluation and one that did not occur."

**Committed next.**
- None declared in the sources read.

**Boundary.** Out of scope: new commerce protocols, internals of any specific commerce system, end-user UI, marketplace design, hardware.

**Author participation.** The author of this map wrote on the public working group mailing list on 2026-10-12, accepting a 30-minute slot, wrote again in the same thread on 2026-10-14, and gave a presentation of the map at the working group call of 2026-10-14; see Disclosure.

### MCP Server Cards (ext-server-card, SEP-2127)
<a id="mcp-server-card"></a>

**AAIF, specification.** Experimental extension of MCP: the README of the extension repository still reads "not an accepted or official MCP extension" (2026-10-13). SEP-2127 (PR #2127, "MCP Server Cards - HTTP Server Discovery", Extensions Track) was merged on 2026-10-06 (14:43 UTC, merge commit 0a11bf6) by a maintainer of the MCP repository; the Specification Enhancement Proposal (SEP) file, read 2026-10-13, carries "Status: Final" and says the full extension specification stays in the extension repository, graduating to a repository named ext-server-card on acceptance. The last commit of the extension repository on main is of 2026-08-12 (PR #47); latest activity read: issue #30 (2026-10-10, 9 comments). (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** A Server Card is "a static metadata document that describes a remote MCP server enough for clients to discover and connect to it"; cards "intentionally omit primitive listings (tools, resources, prompts)". docs/discovery.md says clients performing domain-level discovery SHOULD attempt to retrieve /.well-known/ai-catalog.json, whose entries of type application/mcp-server-card+json point to the cards. Commerce metadata was kept out of the card: in issue #27 (closed 2026-06-13) a member of the modelcontextprotocol organisation (GitHub author association MEMBER, read 2026-10-13) answered "we do not intend to introduce MCP-specific patterns (like `_meta`) into the MCP Catalog"; SEP-2007 (payment support) was closed 2026-06-24 as "dormant". The individual draft draft-morrison-mcp-dns-discovery-09 (Informational, dated 21 October 2026, posted 2026-10-22; text read 2026-11-02) defines DNS discovery for remote MCP servers: a TXT record at `_mcp.<domain>` gives the endpoint URL and transport and optionally an Ed25519 key, which a client checks against the server's TLS certificate, its RFC 9421 response signatures or the key declared in its Server Card (section 5.3.6); "If verification fails, the client MUST treat the server as untrusted". It also defines two other TXT records, `_org-alter.<domain>` and `_alter.<domain>`. It is not adopted by a WG and is not counted among the discovery paths on the home page; its author proposed this addition in issue #22 of this map's repository and states being a co-founder of ~alter, which publishes an `_mcp` record for its own domain.

**Committed next.**
- Roadmap (PR #47, merged 2026-08-12): graduate the Server Card as an official MCP extension, then static primitives (#30) and authentication (#13). (undated) [statement] https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/ext-server-card/pull/47
- Notes of 2026-07-20: "Nested catalog and Link header features deferred to after release." (undated) [statement] https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/discussions/3115

**Boundary.** The README says a Server Card "describes remote connectivity only"; local installation metadata stays in the MCP Registry server.json.

### AI Catalog (Agent Card Working Group)
<a id="lf-ai-catalog"></a>

**Linux Foundation, specification.** Pre-v1 specification in "a temporary working repo maintained by the Linux Foundation", governed by a Technical Steering Committee with four initial seats. The well-known path is optional by design (ADR-0011). On 2026-10-13 the README of main still says "This is a temporary working repo maintained by the Linux Foundation" and "This project will be moved to a permanent location a later date with a permanent governance model."; the last commit of main is of 2026-10-08 (PR #119, implementation classifications); issue #46 (project location) was closed as completed on 2026-10-03 without a comment. PR #110 (did:web verification of signed Entry Trust Manifests) was merged on 2026-10-01. Read 2026-11-09: PR #117, "Support independently signed Trust Manifest contributions", was merged on 2026-10-28; PR #91 (ADR-0021, multiple identities via alsoKnownAs) was closed without merge on 2026-10-29; issue #59 (urn:air naming) is open, with participants agreeing in the thread on 2026-11-05 to use ";". (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** ADR-0011 (accepted 2026-04-02) says a well-known URI "should be registered as a conventional discovery location" but "is not mandatory", so catalogues may be served from any URL. Publisher identity is the most developed theme: urn:air anchored to a domain and did:web verification of signed trust manifests (PR #110, merged 2026-10-01), whose text says "This establishes publisher-domain control, not publisher reputation." Terms of service and privacy policy URLs are fields of the trust manifest (termsOfServiceUrl, privacyPolicyUrl); the maintainers' wording on Agentic Resource Discovery (ARD) is "Documents the decision to loosely couple ARD and AI-Catalog" (PR #77).

**Committed next.**
- Technical Steering Committee (TSC) vote on the urn:air naming (issue #59): "We may require a TSC vote to move forward." (undated) [statement] https://github.com/Agent-Card/ai-catalog/issues/59
- README: "This project will be moved to a permanent location a later date with a permanent governance model." (undated) [normative] https://github.com/Agent-Card/ai-catalog/blob/main/README.md

**Boundary.** Access and monetization signalling is directed to an extension: "I would fully support this as an extension." (issue #83)

**Author participation.** The author of this map commented on PR #117 of the AI Catalog repository on 2026-10-06, on the policy URLs of the extension, and on 2026-10-28, supporting a V0.9 release (PR #117, "Support independently signed Trust Manifest contributions", merged on 2026-10-28); see Disclosure.

### x402
<a id="x402"></a>

**Linux Foundation, specification.** Open payment protocol under the x402 Foundation (Linux Foundation): intent announced 2026-04-02; operational launch and completed contribution by Coinbase announced 2026-07-14, with 40 member organisations at launch. On 2026-10-13 main is at commit 6c470a8 and the core specification header reads "Protocol Version: 2" (specs/x402-specification-v2.md); no release is listed on GitHub (not found, 2026-10-13). The file ROADMAP.md, which read "(update coming soon)" on 2026-10-03, was removed from main on 2026-10-13 by PR #3789, "chore: remove outdated ROADMAP.md and PROJECT-IDEAS.md". Correction: PR #8 of the Domain Discovery working group and PR #2979 of the core repository answered 404 on 2026-11-02, when version 1.3 of this map called them no longer reachable; on 2026-11-09 both are reachable again and open, with the same author; why they were unreachable is not known. Issue #11 of wg-domain-discovery, on the content of `/.well-known/x402`, is open (last comment 2026-10-31); a record of the working group call of 2026-11-02 announced in that thread is not found on GitHub (issue, repository files and the organisation's repositories, 2026-11-09). PR #4 of the working group, which proposes a small entry at `/.well-known/x402` linking to OpenAPI documents, is open. (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** The README says "x402 is an open standard for internet native payments."; the server answers 402 with a PAYMENT-REQUIRED header, the client replies with PAYMENT-SIGNATURE, and an http-message-signatures extension identifies the paying agent. The offer-and-receipt extension lets the server "cryptographically commit to the payment terms it presents in" accepts[] (the payment terms of the offer, not the commercial terms of the sale) and sign a receipt; it says "Wire shape and field placement are not considered stable". The FAQ describes the exact scheme as a push payment, "irreversible once executed". Discovery (seam S1), read 2026-10-23: in the Domain Discovery working group of the x402 Foundation (x402-foundation/wg-domain-discovery) a verifier response schema was merged on 2026-10-02 (PR #6, proposal/verifier-schema.md); PR #8, open since 2026-10-11, proposes `/.well-known/x402` as a single canonical discovery path with a JSON schema, and issue #11, open since 2026-10-14, discusses the shape of that entry and its alignment with x402; in the core repository PR #2979, open since 2026-07-29, proposes DNS and `.well-known` records for facilitators. `/.well-known/x402`: not found in a GitHub code search of x402-foundation/x402 (default branch), 2026-10-23.

**Committed next.**
- None declared in the sources read.

**Boundary.** The FAQ answers "Not natively." on credit-card deposits, placing fiat rails outside the protocol.

### Agent Network Protocol (ANP)
<a id="anp"></a>

**Independent, specification.** Specification set ANP 1.2, GitHub release v1.2 of 2026-09-24 (v1.1 of 2026-06-27, 1.0 of 2025-05-19); the README marks ANP-02, ANP-03, ANP-04, ANP-07 and ANP-08 "Released v1.2", the Messaging 1.2 catalog as published with profile P6 "candidate", and ANP-05, ANP-06 and ANP-10 as drafts. The release notes say "Publication of specifications does not mean implementations already support ANP 1.2". (as of 2026-10-16).

**Relevance to commerce.** ANP-08 defines active discovery at https://{domain}/.well-known/agent-descriptions and passive registration with search agents; agent-descriptions is not found in the IANA well-known URI registry (copy updated 2026-10-07, read 2026-10-14). ANP-07 describes Agent Description documents and lists among its aims "Defining core vocabularies for agent basic information, products, services, interfaces, etc.". ANP-10 is "an ANP application-layer adaptation of the Agent Payment Protocol (AP2)" and says "its presence does not establish stable interoperability, implementation conformance, or production readiness". The protocol draft of the W3C AI Agent Protocol Community Group uses the same discovery path and lists as editor Gaowei Chang, "ANP Open Source Community".

**Committed next.**
- README roadmap item, open: "Complete the registered MLS ExtensionType release gate for stable P6 v2." (undated) [statement] https://github.com/agent-network-protocol/AgentNetworkProtocol/blob/main/README.md

**Boundary.** The v1.2 release notes: "This is a documentation release: it does not change SDK, service, or product implementations, and it does not authorize public capability advertisement."
