### Agentic Resource Discovery (ARD)
<a id="ard"></a>

**Independent, specification.** Specification v0.91 on main (header "Status: Proposal", dated 2026-08-26; header unchanged and last change to spec/ard.md on 2026-10-12, read 2026-10-13); no tag or release in ard-spec (not found, 2026-10-13); Maintainers decide by majority vote; an Oversight Board (Google, Hugging Face, Microsoft, Amazon, Cisco) is published. On 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-12 the maintainers merged PRs #97, #99, #104, #105, #106 and #80 (namespace segments in Appendix C, base URL and search endpoint in section 5.3, ADR 0010, recognized media types in section 3.3, CDDL for explore facets, attestation scopes in section 4.5.2). PR #111, "feat(spec): add informative observability guidance", was merged on 2026-10-25 (commit f073b1e): it adds section 5.5 "Observability (Informative)" to spec/ard.md, non-normative guidance on W3C Trace Context headers for registry operations (search, explore, agents) and on federated registries as trust boundaries (read 2026-11-02). At the IETF, three individual Internet-Drafts of 2026-10-30 (datatracker, read 2026-11-09) take ARD to the DAWN working group: draft-jimenez-dawn-ard-initial-contact-00 (Jiménez, Bu, Arkko; Standards Track) on how a client finds a manifest, through a Service Binding (SVCB) record at `_index._agents.<domain>`, DNS-SD `_ard._tcp`, the well-known URI `ard.json` or the link relation `ard`, following at most one redirect; draft-bu-dawn-ard-discovery-00 on the entries, the Trust Manifest and a federated search interface; and draft-bu-dawn-ard-architecture-00. All three are on the DAWN agenda for IETF 127 (agenda revision of 2026-11-06). Appendix B of the initial-contact draft lists as open issues publishing the manifest as an API catalog profile (RFC 9727), and the underscored DNS labels, since it uses `_index._agents` where the ARD specification lists `_entries._agents` and `_search._agents` (text read 2026-11-04). Implementations declared by third parties in ard-spec issues (read 2026-11-09): allagents.app answers as an ARD registry since 2026-10-29 (issue #112, open, no reply); kansei-link.com publishes a manifest of 28 official MCP servers of Japanese SaaS vendors (issue #109, closed by its author as not planned on 2026-10-16). In spec/schemas/ard-entry.schema.json the property of an entry is spelled `TrustManifest` while the CDDL and the examples use `trustManifest` (main at f073b1e, read 2026-11-04; also noted in issue #110); PR #116, which changes the key, was approved on 2026-11-05 and is not merged on 2026-11-09. Lighthouse PR #17484, merged on 2026-11-03, adds `agentmap` to the directives its robots-txt audit accepts, after issue #17480 reported that the audit flagged the `Agentmap` line that its ard-schema gatherer reads. (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** Agentic Resource Discovery (ARD) defines a manifest of typed entries (A2A agent cards, MCP servers, other resources by media type): "A consumer resolving a domain's entries MUST fetch /.well-known/ard.json"; "a publisher on the predecessor path SHOULD move to ard.json". The chair recorded in issue #61 (2026-07-09): "We decided that at least for now, we will only have a loose coupling between ARD entries and ai-catalog entries." The decision is recorded as ADR 0010 (status Accepted, meeting of 2026-07-09, merged by PR #104 on 2026-10-11): "Loose coupling between ARD and `ai-catalog`". Commerce enters through a "local-business storefront" example (PR #60); issue #20 says "Resources discovered via ARD may require payment or have access restrictions." and "Deferred from v0.9." Lighthouse PR #17295 (merged on 2026-10-09) makes the robots-txt and llms-txt audits fail on HTTP 429, because Google's robots.txt specification makes an exception for 429 "to be treated as a server error"; on PR #17260 (ard-schema) a reviewer wrote on 2026-10-07 "status code 200 is too restrictive. See how other audits (robots-txt, llms-txt) handle status codes."

**Committed next.**
- Lookup by identifier (PR #74, still open on 2026-10-13): "We will merge this into main and ensure it gets synced into the v0.91 draft structure as well." (undated) [statement] https://github.com/ards-project/ard-spec/pull/74
- Neutral host: "a decision for **roughly twelve months out**, not now." (undated) [normative] https://agenticresourcediscovery.org/governance/

**Boundary.** Payment and access signalling were "Deferred from v0.9." (issue #20, still open on 2026-10-13). A maintainer wrote there on 2026-10-11 "With v0.91 and #86 merged, the core mechanism for access and monetization signaling is now in place", carried as namespaced JSON-LD terms rather than payment fields in the base entry; on issue #78 (discovery results and referrals must not confer contact or invocation authority, open) a maintainer wrote "discovery is not authorization" and proposed an editorial clarification in section 3.6 without new normative rules (2026-10-11).

**Author participation.** The author of this map took part in Lighthouse audit issue #17251 on ARD support (closed as completed on 2026-10-09), PR #17260, issue #17271 and PR #17272, and opened PR #100 on ard-spec (merged on 2026-10-11, with a comment of the author on 2026-10-10), Lighthouse Discussion #17441 (2026-10-25, on the llms-txt gatherer and on structured alternates in the Link header) and PR #116 on ard-spec (2026-11-04, the schema key above). The acknowledgments of draft-jimenez-dawn-ard-initial-contact-00 say that "The depth limit and the fixed outcomes follow a discussion on the DAWN list started by Enrique Somoza and continued by Francesco Marinoni Moretto and Balazs Nemethi."; see Disclosure.

### Browser vendor positions on WebMCP (WebKit, Mozilla, Chrome, Edge)
<a id="browsers-webmcp"></a>

**Independent, position.** WebKit position issue #670 closed 2026-06-11 as "position: oppose"; Mozilla #1412 closed 2026-08-05 as "position: neutral". Chrome early preview 2026-02-10; Edge blog of 2026-09-21: implementation "is ready for testing". (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** WebMCP lets a page expose tools to browsing agents; Chrome describes it as "a standard way for exposing structured tools, ensuring AI agents can perform actions on your site with increased speed, reliability, and precision". The WebKit reviewer wrote on 2026-06-03 "WebKit is **opposed** to this proposal due to the following concerns:", among them that "it has no consent or reversibility model for consequential actions". The Mozilla reviewer proposed a neutral position, writing that "in an adversarial setting there is a risk that sites will provide tools that do not match the experience of a user on the page". PayPal's blog of 2026-09-16 says it is "extending WebMCP support through the PayPal JavaScript SDK" for Store Sync merchants; the agent can search, prepare the cart and initiate checkout, and "The buyer is then handed off to PayPal to sign in, choose or confirm a funding source, review the transaction, and approve payment." PayPal reports, as its own results of a small sandbox benchmark: "Approximately 4.6 times less costly in model-inference usage", "Approximately twice as fast at the median", and "three structured tool calls via WebMCP instead of more than 30 interface-interaction steps" (self-reported). Shopify's developer changelog of 2026-09-28 says "Browser agents can now read and update Shopify checkouts using WebMCP tools for checkout", with get_checkout, update_checkout and complete_checkout ("Submit checkout after buyer confirmation"); the tools "don't expose a new API or require merchant configuration". The Checkout WebMCP documentation says "Before you call complete_checkout, show the buyer the current order and total, and get their permission to place it. WBA and ready_for_complete don't grant it."

**Committed next.**
- Mozilla reviewer: "willing to come back to revisit it once there is more evidence of how it will be used by sites" (undated) [statement] https://github.com/mozilla/standards-positions/issues/1412#issuecomment-4593221809

**Boundary.** The positions concern WebMCP and not MCP: the Mozilla reviewer's text says "There is no MCP here."

**Sources.**

- [statement] https://github.com/mozilla/standards-positions/issues/1412 (2026-10-13)

### Agent Payments Protocol (AP2)
<a id="ap2"></a>

**Industry, specification.** Open protocol in the GitHub organisation google-agentic-commerce (Apache-2.0); latest release v0.2.0 of 2026-04-28, which "focuses on providing Human Not Present flows"; core specification donated to FIDO. Google announced the donation on 2026-04-28 ("We're donating Agent Payments Protocol to the FIDO Alliance"); the FIDO release of the same day places the work in its Payments Technical Working Group, where the Google and Mastercard contributions are "providing an initial foundation for these specifications", and announces an Agentic Authentication Technical Working Group. The AP2 documentation (docs/index.md, commit b4587ac of 2026-04-28, the v0.2 release) says "Standardization of the specification will continue within the Agentic Authentication Technical and Payments Technical Working Groups in FIDO."; the FIDO page "Agentic AI" says that FIDO's work in agentic AI "is being carried out in its Agentic Authentication Technical Working Group, and the Payments Technical Working Group", and ties the contributions of Google and Mastercard to the specifications of the Payments Technical Working Group: "These specifications will draw from initial contributions from Google (AP2) and Mastercard (Verifiable Intent)" (both read 2026-10-23). (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** AP2 is available as an extension for A2A and for UCP; its objects are mandates, signed Verifiable Digital Credentials, and the FIDO blog of 2026-05-26 names "Checkout Mandate: This captures the details about and conditions on what the user wants to buy." and "Payment Mandate : This captures the details about and conditions on how the user wants to pay (amount, instrument, timing)." The docs say the Checkout Mandate is shared with the merchant, so the seller is a recipient of the user's signed purchase terms. The record the other way, the Mandate Receipt that the Verifier signs, is described with a `result` field in docs/ap2/agent_authorization.md and with a `status` field in the normative schemas, at the same commit e1ea56d (see issue #374 in the open proposals). UCP's AP2 mandates extension has businesses embed a signature over the checkout terms.

**Committed next.**
- Standardisation continues in the FIDO Agentic Authentication and Payments Technical Working Groups (repository docs) (undated) [statement] https://github.com/google-agentic-commerce/AP2/blob/main/docs/index.md
- The FIDO Alliance will provide reports as its agentic workstreams progress (undated) [decision] https://fidoalliance.org/fido-alliance-to-develop-standards-for-trusted-ai-agent-interactions/

**Boundary.** The docs index (docs/index.md at commit e1ea56d, the head of main on 2026-10-13) says the initial version supports pull payment methods such as credit and debit cards and that the roadmap includes e-wallets, push payments such as real-time bank transfers, and digital currencies; docs/index.md describes "the initial version" as supporting these payment methods, and the latest release is v0.2.0 (2026-04-28).

### Agentic Commerce Protocol (ACP)
<a id="acp"></a>

**Industry, specification.** Specification in beta, maintained by OpenAI and Stripe; MAINTAINERS.md also lists Meta (PR #236 "ADMIN: Add Meta as TSC member", merged 2026-04-24). Last dated version 2026-04-17; on 2026-10-13 main is still at commit 7fdd78d (2026-07-17), README badge "Beta", no tag or release (not found, 2026-10-13), and the directory spec/unreleased holds the changes not yet dated (checkout, cart, delegate, feed schemas and OpenAPI files). (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** The README says ACP "is an interaction model and open standard for connecting buyers, their AI agents, and businesses to complete purchases seamlessly." and covers agentic checkout and delegated payment over Representational State Transfer (REST) and an MCP binding. The seller enters through the checkout session's required links field (terms, privacy, return, shipping) and the unreleased product feed, whose optional barcode has a type (GTIN, UPC or EAN) and a value; requests carry a Signature header; the order schema has a receipt_url. The discovery RFC says "Seller identity is established through authentication (Bearer token), which is unavailable at discovery time." The OpenAI developer documentation (developers.openai.com/commerce/specs/checkout, read 2026-10-14) is a text separate from the specification in the repository (spec/unreleased). It says "Building with the Agentic Commerce Protocol is open to all." and "Instant Checkout in ChatGPT is currently available to approved partners." It documents a cancel call for a checkout session, which answers 405 when the session is already canceled or completed, and an order object with a list of refunds (types store_credit and original_payment); the cancellation of a checkout session is thus not the cancellation or refund of a completed order. The page /commerce/specs/delegated-payment answered HTTP 404 on 2026-10-14.

**Committed next.**
- README future path: "Neutral foundation stewardship as ecosystem matures"; no date (undated) [statement] https://github.com/agentic-commerce-protocol/agentic-commerce-protocol/blob/main/README.md

**Boundary.** The discovery document states "Agent identity and authorization are separate concerns", leaving catalog discovery and agent registration outside it.

It says EMVCo has established a dedicated Agentic Payments Task Force and collaborates with the FIDO Alliance, the OpenID Foundation, the OpenWallet Foundation and W3C.

### KYAPay (Skyfire)
<a id="kyapay"></a>

**Industry, specification.** Individual Internet-Drafts named for the OAuth Working Group (WG) (draft-skyfire-oauth-*); WG stream or adoption: not found on the datatracker, 2026-10-13 and again 2026-11-09 (kyapay-token and using-kyapay-tokens are listed under "Individual Submissions" with no stream). Current revisions, read 2026-11-09: kyapay-token-03 and using-kyapay-tokens-02, both published on the datatracker on 2026-11-02. On 2026-10-02 the author announced new revisions of five drafts: kyapay-token-02, using-kyapay-tokens-01, amr-values-02, id-verification-02 and aml-methods-01 (datatracker date 2026-10-01 for all five). The datatracker lists two more documents of the family, kyapay-token-exchange-01 (2026-07-19) and kyapayprofile-02 (2026-06-20, replaced by kyapay-token), seven in all. The Technical Plenary and Advisory Committee (TPAC) breakout proposal #39 (KYAPay: Verified Agent Identity and Payments for Ubiquitous Agentic Commerce) is open on 2026-10-13 with one comment and no assigned slot; the datatracker shows no revision of kyapay-token-exchange after -01 of 2026-07-19. (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** KYAPay defines a signed JWT carrying verified agent and human identity (KYA) and payment credentials (PAY), so that merchant security infrastructure can distinguish human-authorised agents from malicious bots. The author's announcement of 2026-10-02 describes the token as a "JWT token providing claims about the person or organization authorizing an agent to act, the agent platform, the agent instance, and optionally a payment method". On the oauth list the token is discussed among bot managers, CDNs and site policy engines.

**Committed next.**
- The author invites review of the updated drafts on the oauth list and at IETF 127 (14 to 20 November 2026). (undated) [third party] https://mailarchive.ietf.org/arch/msg/oauth/h_YQPuYbBQf0TaiNIJQDNcxgGfc/
- Rework of the delegation text of the token-exchange draft, stated by the author on 2026-09-18. (undated) [third party] https://mailarchive.ietf.org/arch/msg/oauth/CbHD0qu_C-7AgMj3OdM7QNmbRjk

**Boundary.** A participant review of 2026-09-23 reads hid, aid and apd as identifying principal, agent and platform, not the authority delegated.

### Mastercard Agent Pay
<a id="mastercard-agent-pay"></a>

**Industry, specification.** Commercial programme of Mastercard for agent-initiated payments, described on its framework page (read in a browser on 2026-10-14; first read 2026-10-02). Page metadata: datePublished 2025-10-14, dateModified 2025-11-04. The page introduces the Mastercard Agent Pay Acceptance Framework: registration and verification of agents before they transact with agentic tokens, Web Bot Auth at the content delivery network (CDN) layer, and a Dynamic Token Verification Code, an agentic token formatted for standard card payment fields, submitted in existing checkout forms. The page refers to developer.mastercard.com for use cases; that site was not read. (as of 2026-10-14).

**Relevance to commerce.** The framework page (a Mastercard statement) says "By implementing the emerging Web Bot Auth standard (as outlined in the framework) at the Content Delivery Network (CDN) layer, merchants can verify agent authenticity", and that "we’ve partnered with Cloudflare to support Web Bot Auth in Mastercard Agent Pay". Mastercard is the maintainer of Verifiable Intent (see verifiable-intent).

### Personal Agent Consent & Trust Protocol (PACT), published by Decagon with Instinct
<a id="pact"></a>

**Industry, specification.** Version 1.0 of the specification ("This is PACT 1.0.") is published at openpactprotocol.org (the Decagon announcement is dated 2026-10-06), and the repository openpactprotocol/openpactprotocol (created 2026-10-01, Apache-2.0) holds the documentation, a reference Provider, a demo personal agent and conformance tests; its latest commit on main is 838c6bd (2026-10-06, a dependency update), read 2026-10-13. Decagon, 2026-10-06: it is open-sourcing PACT, "co-developed and supported by Instinct". No standards body or governing organisation is named in the pages of the site read on 2026-10-13. (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** PACT builds on A2A 1.0 and defines how a personal agent identifies itself to a business's support agent and, optionally, which permissions the customer has granted it. The business's Agent Card is found at "https://{brandDomain}/.well-known/agent-card.json", which "serves the card or redirects to" the Provider's URL; the personal agent "never builds it from a Brand ID". The Poppy protocol uses a different well-known document (see pap and seam S1). The personal agent signs every request with a JWT (ES256 or RS256) checked against its published keys, and section 3.3 says what that proves: "That a known personal agent is calling for someone it calls sub. Not that sub owns a Brand account." Delegated authority is optional: the customer logs in with the business, never with the personal agent, and approves scopes that "Each Brand defines" (examples on the card: orders:read, orders:cancel), through OAuth 2.0 device code (RFC 8628). The Decagon post puts it as "It separates the identity of the personal agent from its authority to act on a customer". Section 5.6: every reply under a delegation token carries a signed receipt (grant id, user, scopes used, actions, timestamp), and "Personal agents SHOULD verify and keep receipts". The receipt records what the Provider's agent did; the words consumer, durable medium and confirm are not found in the specification page (2026-10-13).

**Committed next.**
- None declared in the sources read.

**Boundary.** The specification does not define a registry: "This spec does not define a registry." Registration of a personal agent with a Provider is out of scope ("How these are exchanged is out of scope"), and each Brand defines its own scopes, none reserved by PACT.

**Sources.**
- [normative] https://openpactprotocol.org/spec (2026-10-13)
- [statement] https://openpactprotocol.org/ (2026-10-13)
- [normative] https://github.com/openpactprotocol/openpactprotocol (2026-10-13)
- [statement] https://decagon.ai/blog/introducing-the-personal-agent-consent-trust-protocol-pact (2026-10-13)

**Overlaps:** [pap](#pap), [a2a-core](#a2a-core), [a2a-discovery](#a2a-discovery)

**Coverage:** see [Evidence](#evidence-pact).

### Personal Agent Protocol (PAP, "Poppy"), published by Sierra with Meta
<a id="pap"></a>

**Industry, specification.** Draft 0.1 of the specification is published at personalagentprotocol.org (announced 2026-10-06, draft published 2026-10-09; the pages read on 2026-10-13 are marked "Updated Oct 12, 2026"), under the Apache License 2.0 with the names and the logo excluded; a further 35 design partners were named on 2026-10-09. Governance is worded two ways, not reconciled here. Sierra, 2026-10-06: "an open standard Meta and Sierra are developing along with industry partners at Genesys, Instinct, Rocket, Shopify, Stripe, and Walmart". Decagon, 2026-10-06: "join the Personal Agent Protocol working group announced by the team behind Meta's Muse to help shape an industry standard". No standards body or governing organisation is named in the pages of the site read on 2026-10-13. Read 2026-11-09, as of 2026-11-06: in the specification's repository (personal-agent-protocol/personal-agent-protocol, created 2026-10-14) version 0.2 was published on 2026-10-26 and version 0.3 on 2026-11-05, which opened draft 0.4; a reference implementation is not published by 2026-11-06, and the Demos page at the commit that published 0.3 lists demos "built by the community" that "have not been officially reviewed or endorsed by the Personal Agent Protocol authors or partners"; a public record of design workshops is not found (2026-11-09). Sierra's landing page now says "40+ design partners". (as of 2026-11-09).

**Relevance to commerce.** Discovery is one well-known document: "A Company MUST publish its poppy.json document at https://{domain}/.well-known/poppy.json over HTTPS". The well-known URL "MAY redirect" to where the document is hosted, and "Personal Agents MUST follow redirects, and every redirect MUST be to an HTTPS URL"; the UCP specification says the opposite for its profile (see seam S1). poppy.json is not in the Internet Assigned Numbers Authority (IANA) well-known URI registry (not found in the registry XML, 2026-10-13). The agent is identified by a URL: "A Personal Agent's client_id MUST be an HTTPS URL that returns its metadata document", which carries a jwks_uri and a token_endpoint_auth_method of private_key_jwt; the Company can register, allow-list, block or revoke client ids. The operations extension, section 8 ("What a confirmation proves"), says what a confirmation does not show: "It doesn't prove that the User saw the terms or approved them." The announcement of 2026-10-06 says the protocol "defines how personal agents interact with businesses": it "starts on the website, where a personal agent can discover what the company offers and how to reach it", the session "is built on OAuth", and the customer decides whether the agent has read-only or write access. Context, not part of the protocol: Retail Insight Network reported on 2026-09-23 that Shopify and Meta partnered so that Meta's assistant Muse can complete purchases across Shopify's merchants through Shop Pay.

**Committed next.**
- Design workshops and a reference implementation "over the next month" (Sierra blog, 2026-10-09); on 2026-11-06 the reference implementation was not published and no public record of the workshops was found. (approx. 2026-11-09) [statement] https://sierra.ai/blog/poppy

**Boundary.** The Path to 1.0 page lists as areas "the draft doesn't cover yet": payments ("How a personal agent makes payments in a way that fits the protocol, including how that works with existing payment protocols."), push notifications and attachments ("such as receipts, return labels, product images, or forms for the user to fill in").

- [third party] https://www.implicator.ai/meta-sierra-personal-agent-protocol/ (2026-10-08)
- [third party] https://www.retail-insight-network.com/news/shopify-meta-deal-ai-agent-shopping/ (2026-10-08)

### Universal Commerce Protocol (UCP)
<a id="ucp"></a>

**Industry, specification.** Read 2026-11-09, as of Friday 2026-11-06. Releases: the latest is v2026-08-25 (2026-08-25); none follows on GitHub or in the versions list of ucp.dev. Lodging: a working draft merged on main on 2026-09-24 (PR #780, commit c9a1395, page marked "Draft - Work in Progress"), not yet in a published release (ucp.dev/latest/specification/lodging/booking/ answers 404, the draft path 200); issue #858, which lists its open design questions, carries the milestone 2026-12-08, the next version cut. Merged on main since the last release, among others: the Common capability `ask` (PR #538, 2026-10-07), the return policy extension (PR #634, 2026-10-23) and an Identity Linking rule permitting email matching when the issuer is the mailbox authority (PR #998, 2026-10-31, commit 58c7ab9). Open on 2026-11-06: the feed capability for bulk catalog access (PR #866, ready for review since 2026-10-29, "[DRAFT]" dropped from its title on 2026-11-06; commits of 2026-11-04 on its branch clarify record conformance for custom record types and authentication for feed-only publishers); purchase options and subscription policy (PR #890, approved by three reviewers, based on the branch of PR #987, not on main); food checkout (PR #897, approved on 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05); business identity and seller attribution (PRs #939 and #1017 to #1020, re-pushed together on 2026-11-06); product attributes shaped like schema.org PropertyValue (issue #400 and PR #401, open since 2026-04-30 and 2026-05-01; no comment by a Tech Council member found in their timelines). Governance (repository .github: GOVERNANCE.md at commit 258bf34, TC_ELECTIONS.md at 042867b; minutes in the repository meeting-minutes at commit 6d1b764): four Domain Tech Councils (DTC) of 16 members each. Shopping: 4 permanent members each from Google and Shopify (8 votes) and 8 members from any organization. Food: permanent members from Google (6), DoorDash, Toast, Square and Uber Eats (10 votes) and 6 from any organization. Lodging: Google (6), Amadeus, Booking.com, Expedia, Hilton, Marriott and Trip.com (12 votes) and 4 from any organization. Payments: Google (3), Shopify (3), Adyen NV, Ant International, Coinbase, Global Payments, PayPal and Stripe (12 votes) and 4 from any organization. Elected seats are "open for re-election every six months, in June and December", and the Governing Council (GC) "may decide to conduct elections at any time if there are open seats". Minutes published by 2026-11-06: Shopping and Payments (four Payments minutes, 2026-09-24 to 2026-10-22, although the Payments folder README still reads "No minutes have been published yet."); none for Food and Lodging (the first Lodging minutes, of 2026-11-03, were merged on 2026-11-07). New councils: the charters of Local Services (issue #922) and B2B Procurement (issue #947) were approved by the GC on 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05; nominations for them had not been opened by 2026-11-06. Domain Working Groups (DWG): Bulk Product Discovery (issue #682, from which PR #866) and Agentic Donations (issue #924, still labelled gov:needs-gc-review; on 2026-11-05 the GC wrote that its chairs can invite nominations); two Payments DWG charters were proposed on 2026-11-05, Buy Now, Pay Later (issue #1049) and Card Network Tokenization (issue #1050, "[DRAFT]" in its title), without labels. The GC minutes of 2026-10-30, merged on 2026-11-06, record the approval of the charters of #924 and #922. (as of 2026-11-06).

**Relevance to commerce.** The AP2 mandates extension says businesses must "embed a cryptographic signature in checkout responses, proving the terms (price, line items) are authentic" and platforms provide mandates "proving the user explicitly authorized the specific checkout state and funds transfer". The Order page calls the entity "the authoritative latest state of the order at the time of retrieval or delivery", where webhook payloads "be signed by the business and verified by the platform". Terms travel in policies[] (PR #572, merged 2026-07-23), where the business states rules such as a refund window; the PR says "Policies never force display." On agent identity, the signatures page (PR #483, merged 2026-07-03) says a dual-audience signature "is the RECOMMENDED path for integrators" who want interoperability with Web Bot Auth verifiers; that interoperability is request-scoped. A food domain (`dev.ucp.food.checkout`, `food.fulfillment`) was proposed on 2026-10-03 for early feedback (PR #897, opened by a participant "On behalf of the entire Food Domain Tech Council"; its label on 2026-10-05 was status:needs-triage and on 2026-10-09 and 2026-10-13 is status:under-review, with gov:needs-tc-review and 12 comments on 2026-10-13). A proposal to authenticate the platform client through attestation (issue #852, opened 2026-09-22) was described by its author on 2026-10-04 as "now ready for Technical Council review". Issue #375 (2026-05-29) declined external place identifiers in the core schema: "Adding specialized reconciliation mappings to the core schema is a premature optimization." The Common capability `dev.ucp.common.ask` was merged on 2026-10-07 (PR #538; commit a41a2dc). Its page says "The Ask capability provides natural-language Q&A about a Business's resources, policies, and services." and "`ask` is the open-question complement to UCP's structured commerce capabilities". Two rules of its text: "A Business MUST NOT change the state of any resource exposed through another UCP capability or extension in response to an `ask` request." and "An answer is indicative, not authoritative."; where an answer conflicts with the representation returned by the capability that owns the resource, "that representation is authoritative and the Platform MUST prefer it", and "A Platform MUST NOT treat an `answer` as the binding disclosure for a safety, allergen, or regulatory claim." The page lists as typical use cases product questions ("fit, materials, compatibility, comparisons"), policy questions ("returns, shipping, warranty, refunds"), FAQ and how-to ("store hours, shipping timelines, accepted payment methods") and clarification about a specific resource, grounded in an identifier. The description of the pull request, not the specification, lists "policies, fit and suitability, materials, compatibility, amenities, support information, and Business-specific knowledge". The merge adds docs/specification/common/ask/ (index, Representational State Transfer (REST) and MCP bindings), docs/specification/shopping/ask/index.md and source/schemas/common/ask.json; text read at commit f2206e6 (the head of main on 2026-10-09; on 2026-10-13 main is at 9756aa9, and a git diff of the two commits shows no change to the overview, AP2 mandates, signatures, order, ask and lodging pages cited here). Pull request #811 (opened 2026-09-07 by a participant, open; among its labels on 2026-10-09 status:stale-review and gov:needs-tc-review; no comment or review; state and labels unchanged on 2026-10-13) proposes an optional `offers[]` array on the catalog variant "so one catalog can list a product sold by several different merchants and send each to the right seller's checkout". Each offer names its seller by `seller.domain`, the Merchant of Record, with the seller's own `item_id` and an authoritative price; the proposed schema text describes the domain as "The seller's canonical host: a bare hostname without scheme, path, port, or trailing slash" and adds "A Platform resolves this host to the seller's UCP profile at `/.well-known/ucp` and verifies its signing keys via authority binding." The overview page uses "canonical" for the host that serves a schema (lines 158 to 164); which of the bare and the www host is canonical for a profile is not found in the overview page at f2206e6 nor in the five files of the pull request (2026-10-09). The overview page says, in Profile Requirements, Hosting, item 2, "Profile endpoints MUST NOT use redirects (3xx)." (line 1466) and, in the rules for any URL dereferenced during identity resolution, item 2, "Implementations MUST NOT follow redirects (3xx)." (line 1514); line 182 applies the same rule to schema URLs (file at commit f2206e6 of 2026-10-07; unchanged at 9756aa9 on 2026-10-13). Items 3 and 4 of Hosting ask for a Cache-Control with public and a max-age of at least 60 seconds, and for a validator (ETag or Last-Modified). The well-known URI suffix `ucp` is registered in the IANA Well-Known URIs registry, status provisional, registered 2026-10-26, change controller Ilya Grigorik, reference the UCP overview (profile structure) (registry updated 2026-10-26, read 2026-10-27). The registration request (issue #92 of the registry's repository, opened 2026-06-06) was closed as completed on 2026-10-27. The profile rule first appears in commit 0426800 of 2026-02-17, "feat: signing for UCP requests & responses (#156)". The author of the census reported in issue #904 (2026-10-07, 2026-10-08) and Discussion #867 (comment 18810486, posted 2026-10-08) a census of 225 hosts probed from Seoul, one route and one attempt per host, 3xx not followed. Version of 2026-10-08, read in the edit history of the comment (figures declared by the source, not verified by this map): 62 positives (200, parses as JSON, ucp.version present), of which 58 Shopify and 4 not identified; declared versions 2026-08-25 (58), 2026-04-08 (3), 2026-01-23 (1); signing keys published by 0 of 62; the other rows: 1 refused by robots.txt, 7 responses 200 that did not parse, 12 3xx at the profile path, 65 404, 2 410, 59 403, 3 429/418/503, 14 connect, timeout or TLS failures. The author states "Not a random draw": a hand-built list of well-known storefronts plus the WooCommerce showcase, heavily Shopify-skewed, so the rate is not an adoption estimate; all figures declared by the source, not verified by this map. Four of the 62 positives have a home page answering 403 or 429 while the profile path answers 200 (comment of 2026-10-07, where the hosts are named). The author reports that probing the host as written gave 60 positives and 23 3xx, and that resolving the canonical host first gave 62 and 12. In the frame of #867 the 62 is "row 2 and ucp.version present", that is a floor on row 2 as the frame defines it (parses as JSON), with rows 3 and 4 not measured; the author of this map replied that the 7 non-parsing 200s are row 1 without row 2, whereas the run lists them among the cases it "could not ask". In Discussion #864 (Lodging), a participant who operates a small property asked on 2026-10-07 how a platform finds a property that is not in a global distribution system (GDS), a chain central reservation system (CRS) or a channel manager feed; the author of this map replied on 2026-10-08 that "today a property-hosted profile is necessary but not sufficient", and the same participant asked whether platforms will discover a profile on the property's own domain or receive lodging inventory only through connectivity partners; no maintainer reply was found among the discussion's six comments, the last of 2026-10-08 (read 2026-10-13). The lodging booking page says a platform "MUST supply valid property.id, and either a pre-composed stay.id OR both accommodation_type.id and rate_plan.id identifiers sourced from upper-funnel discovery mechanisms" (lines 426 to 428, commit f2206e6); the location_summary schema describes id as "Stable, opaque, Business-scoped Location identifier." Issue #678 (buyer consent at complete_checkout) was closed on 2026-10-07 with the merge of PR #883, and issue #666 (permalink and authenticated buyer session) the same day with the merge of PR #789, "require explicit buyer action before cart merge". In PR #691 (price_treatment, open) the author wrote on 2026-10-07 that after dropping not_asserted "an absent price_treatment and an absent tax now mean the same thing everywhere". Method note for censuses of /.well-known/ucp: WooCommerce PR #70679, merged on 2026-11-05 (milestone 11.4.0), fixes a discovery bug introduced by the PR that added the profile (#69886, merged 2026-10-16): requests other than GET and HEAD now get 405 with an Allow header, and the rewrite rule is checked on each request so that a store whose feature flag was set without the usual action no longer answers 404; PR #70680 (open on 2026-11-06) would serve the profile on stores using plain permalinks. A census run before these fixes reach a store may count a store with UCP enabled as having no profile; the release that ships them is not verified here. WooCommerce PR #69577 (open, draft on 2026-10-08) adds UCP catalog routes for search, lookup and product, implementing spec version 2026-04-08, off by default behind the woocommerce_ucp_enabled filter. Known Good posted in Discussion #867 on 2026-10-08, with the figures of https://knowngood.sh/standard/ucp, a run of 2026-09-26 to 2026-09-28 from AWS us-east-1 with robots.txt honoured and the user agent KnownGood-Verifier/0.3 (figures declared by the source, not verified by this map). Its population is 92,541 hosts that its crawl was allowed to ask, from its own index, "not a draw from storefronts". Rows of the frame as reported: HTTP 200 on 45,362 hosts (44,118 at the first request and 1,244 after a redirect), parses as JSON on 42,757, row 3 not measured ("Row 3 is empty on purpose."), and a protocol response (tools/list on the MCP endpoint named by the profile) on 41,424. By endpoint domain: myshopify.com 31,568, wixapis.com 9,782 and three singletons; "Merchant count 41,424; decision count 2" (two tool signatures cover 41,352 endpoints). The Known Good page says "Shopify and Wix advertise the MCP endpoint they run for every store through this profile". No page of Wix on UCP support was found (two searches, 2026-10-09), so Wix is a platform observed by Known Good only. The run follows 3xx at the profile path ("3xx followed up to 2 hops, https only, same registrable domain only"): 2,546 3xx, 2,188 followed, 1,244 ended in 200, and 286 cross-domain 3xx not followed; line 1514 of the overview page reads "Implementations MUST NOT follow redirects (3xx)." The 1,244 are reported here apart from the first-request count. Other figures declared by the source, not verified by this map: 42,434 profiles declare a checkout capability (a declaration, not a transaction); ucp.version declared 2026-08-25 on 41,624, 2026-04-08 on 898 and none on 120; 925 of the parsing profiles are on hosts whose home page was not 2xx. Could not ask, site side: robots.txt 102, 401 and 403 1,350, 402 141, 5xx 157, TLS 5, connection refused 12, reset 6 (sum 1,773, computed here); prober side: 429 504, timeouts 104, per-host budget 89 and 31, other 3 (sum 731, computed here). The authors report two defects in their own record: "Our probe stored the profile's top-level version, not ucp.version", with counts re-read from the stored bodies, and, in their words, "on 1,570 hosts the per-host budget ran out inside the capability loop, so 1,147 hosts that answered 200 kept no result beyond row 1", so "row 2 is a floor by that much". The comment was edited on 2026-10-09 as a re-run of that day restructured against the frame, and its author wrote the same day (comment 18831816) that it was updated against the three points. Figures of the 9 October run (declared by the source, not verified by this map): 225 hosts and 675 requests (225 storefront requests to find the host the storefront settles on, 225 profile-path requests and 225 robots.txt requests), user agent string agentbi/0.1 (the prober's own), vantage KR (Seoul, AS45102); row 1 (HTTP 200) 68 hosts, row 2 and ucp.version present 62 (the source counted the two conditions as one figure until its run of 13 Oct, see below), rows 3 and 4 not measured, no validator run. Other outcomes of that run: 404 64, 410 2, 3xx at the profile path 12 (8 same-host protocol or path redirects, 4 cross-host with the Location host recorded), 200 that does not parse 6, which the author counts as "row 1 without row 2"; could not ask, site side: 403 59, robots.txt refusal 1, 5xx 1, TLS failure 1, connection refused or DNS 2, other 4xx (418) 1; prober side: timeouts 10, 429 1 ("Retry-After not recorded; that is a gap on my side"), URLs not sent because of the prober's own server-side request forgery (SSRF) guard 3. On 2026-10-09 the author of this map posted version 3 of the frame in Discussion #867 (comment 18833761), after the two full runs of issue #904 and of Known Good. It adds a run field for the probed host with the counts "seed host as written, www-normalised, other host in the same registrable domain"; a line for 3xx at the profile path, since "a profile reached only via a 3xx is never a positive"; a row 1a, "200 after redirect", counted "only if 3xx were followed; non-conforming, excluded from 2-4"; a prober-side line widened to "anything the prober chose not to send or could not finish"; and an optional hosting row H, "of row 2: Cache-Control public, max-age ≥ 60; ETag or Last-Modified". The comment ties the redirect rows to the two rules of the overview page quoted above (lines 1466 and 1514) and the hosting row to items 3 and 4 of the same Hosting list. The author of the census answered the same day (comment 18834311) "this is how I'll report the next pass." (his figures, declared by the source, not verified by this map): for the 225 seeds the probed host is the seed host as written for 201, www-normalised for 14 and another host of the same registrable domain for 10; 3xx at the profile path are 8 same-host, 3 same registrable domain and 1 other domain, none counted positive; row 1a is 0 "by construction" because the run never follows a 3xx at the profile path; Retry-After is now recorded and the hosting row measurable in the next pass. On 2026-10-10 and 2026-10-11 the thread of Discussion #867 went on. The author of the census re-ran on 2026-10-11 (comment 18860202; figures declared by the source, not verified by this map): 225 hosts, vantage KR, row 2 and ucp.version present on 61 hosts (62 on 9 Oct; one host moved from a 200 to a 403, so from positive to "could not ask, site side"), and none of the three hosts that had settled on an asset host on 9 Oct did so again; the hosting row was first measured, with 58 of the 61 positives sending Cache-Control public with max-age of at least 60 and 4 sending ETag or Last-Modified. He adopted the case "storefront did not settle on a storefront host" (comment 18861115) and will publish beside the headline how many positives depend on the fallback from 2026-10-12. Known Good split its run by row 1a (comment 18866655; declared): of the 1,244 answers of 200 after a redirect, 4 parse and none is among the 41,424 protocol responses, so 0 of them depend on following a redirect; the bucket of hosts that did not settle on a storefront host (a) is 451 hosts, 80 of them protocol responses, all platform-supplied (myshopify.com 62, wixapis.com 18); his later comment gives the vantage as AWS us-east-1, one egress address. The author of this map posted the updated frame on 2026-10-11 (comment 18867673), "the version to copy", with the new case, the probed and the settled host per row, and the vantage as a run field. No run by anyone else was posted after 2026-10-11 in the 11 comments read on 2026-10-13. On 2026-10-14 (03:00 UTC, comment 18913822; figures declared by the source, not verified by this map) the author of the census wrote that "row 2: 61" reads as though 61 were row 2 as the frame defines it (parses as JSON) and that it is not: the figure is 200, parses as JSON and ucp.version declared, so a floor on row 2; the two numbers are reported apart from the run of 13 Oct, and are 61 and 61 on 13 and 14 Oct (difference 0); on 14 Oct 67 hosts answered 200 at the path, 61 parse as JSON, 5 are HTML soft-404s and 1 answers 200 with a body that is neither; row 3 and row 4 are not measured. On 2026-10-08 a participant working in hotel revenue management proposed restrictions on the stay object of the lodging draft and wrote that the current standard errors "would likely return inventory_exhausted, which gives the agent nothing to recover with"; a hotel operator added "+1 from a six-unit property" (comment 18822183). The Standard Errors table of the lodging booking page (lines 384 to 389) lists four codes, inventory_exhausted, occupancy_exceeded_capacity, payment_failed and eligibility_invalid, and describes inventory_exhausted as "The selected accommodation unit or inventory hold is no longer available"; no code for rate or stay restrictions is listed there (main at f2206e6, read 2026-10-09). Google's own developer pages, describing Google's product built on UCP (class statement; distinct from the standard's text): The lodging FAQ (https://developers.google.com/hotels/ucp/faq, "Last updated 2026-09-24 UTC") says "This first milestone unlocks Booking", "The best way to provide feedback is through GitHub, where the protocol is published." and "Over the coming months, Google will adopt this standard to power its hotel booking feature on AI Mode."; on ranking it answers "No. Opting for our booking integration does not influence how we rank your properties or rates in listings." The lodging page (https://developers.google.com/hotels/ucp, no update date shown on the page read) has a waitlist ("Join the waitlist"), "Conceptual mock only." and "Detailed onboarding and specs coming soon." The food ordering page (https://developers.google.com/actions-center/verticals/ordering/ucp, no update date shown on the page read) has a waitlist, "Detailed onboarding and specs coming soon." and "You remain the Merchant of Record."; allergens, dietary information, nutrition, disclosures, notices and alcohol are not mentioned on the page read on 2026-10-09. On 2026-10-30 Kin Lane (API Evangelist) posted in Discussion #867 a profile-only census of the APIs.io catalog, run that day: 27,662 distinct hosts; at /.well-known/ucp 3,207 answered 200, of which 2,484 also on a nonsense control path, and 477 returned a JSON object carrying a `ucp` object (425 Shopify, 1 Wix, 52 self-hosted by the poster's classification); robots.txt was not consulted in that run. Figures declared by the source, not verified by this map; the weekly files at apis.io/standards/measurements are published under CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 and are cited here, not incorporated.

**Committed next.**
- Lodging booking capability: issue #858, the design questions left by PR #780, carries the milestone 2026-12-08, the version cut of the release train. (2026-12-08) [decision] https://github.com/Universal-Commerce-Protocol/ucp/issues/858
- Lodging roadmap (PR #860, merged 2026-09-25): "beyond Booking, supporting hotels discovery, room selection, rate options, loyalty, discounts" (undated) [decision] https://github.com/Universal-Commerce-Protocol/ucp/pull/860

**Boundary.** PR #780 declares out of scope "Upstream lodging search/discovery service."

**Author participation.** The author of this map took part in PR #866, issues #550 and #858, Discussions #864 and #867 (opened by the author; replies on 2026-10-10, 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-23, and the reporting frame updated on 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-23), PR #890, PR #897 (comments from 2026-10-09 to 2026-10-13, and on 2026-10-23), PR #964 (a comment on 2026-10-19), PR #966 (comments on 2026-10-19 and 2026-10-21 on the `not_accepted` return eligibility wording; on 2026-10-22 at 04:01 UTC the PR author thanked the author of this map and another reviewer, wrote that he had pushed an update, and merged the PR into the branch of PR #634) and issue #904. From 2026-10-23 to 2026-11-02 the author also commented on issue #924 (offering to contribute to the technical report; the author filed a nomination as an independent domain expert), PR #890, issue #957, PR #939, PR #897 (review comments on 2026-10-28 and 2026-10-29, on allergen disclosure after review, tip defaults and withdrawal), PR #866 (on 2026-10-29, on `unit_price` in supplemental feeds; commit 7f1161e on the PR branch, 2026-10-30, "docs(feed): clarify unit price updates in supplements"), Discussion #867 (2026-10-27 and 2026-10-31), PR #1017 and PR #1020 (2026-11-02), and opened Discussion #999 (2026-10-27) on the IANA registration. After 2026-11-02: a comment on PR #1033 (lodging guest fields, 2026-11-04, on nationality declared at booking) and a comment on issue #858 (2026-11-07, after the Friday of version 1.4, on accommodation types); as a nominee to the Agentic Donations DWG (issue #924, as an independent domain expert) the author attended its first meeting on 2026-11-05 (by his statement); see Disclosure.

### Verifiable Intent
<a id="verifiable-intent"></a>

**Industry, specification.** Draft v0.1 specification, maintained by Mastercard according to its README (Apache-2.0, tag v0.1.0). The FIDO Alliance release of 2026-04-28 says Mastercard has contributed it to the Payments Technical Working Group. On 2026-10-13 the last commit on main is of 2026-04-20 (356c296, PR #23), v0.1.0 is the only tag, no release is listed, and the README header reads "Draft (v0.1). Maintained by Mastercard; open to multi-stakeholder contribution." (as of 2026-10-13).

**Relevance to commerce.** The repository describes it as an "Open specification for cryptographic agent authorization in commerce"; the README defines a "layered SD-JWT credential format" giving cryptographic evidence that an agent's actions stayed within the scope the user delegated. Layers hold mandates (signed claims on checkout details or payment parameters) and user constraints; the merchant receives the checkout mandate (layer 3b, Autonomous mode). The FIDO release says "Mastercard has contributed its Verifiable Intent framework, co-developed with Google and designed to work with AP2"; Google as co-developer: not found in the repository pages read, 2026-10-02.

**Committed next.**
- None declared in the sources read.

**Boundary.** Out of scope in the README: transport, key management, provider enrolment, agent platform APIs, dispute resolution, regulatory compliance mapping.

**Sources.**
- [normative] https://github.com/agent-intent/verifiable-intent/blob/main/README.md (2026-10-13)
- [normative] https://github.com/agent-intent/verifiable-intent/blob/main/spec/design-rationale.md (2026-10-02)
- [decision] https://fidoalliance.org/fido-alliance-to-develop-standards-for-trusted-ai-agent-interactions/ (2026-10-03)

**Overlaps:** [mastercard-agent-pay](#mastercard-agent-pay), [ucp](#ucp), [emvco-agentic-payments-framework](#emvco-agentic-payments-framework), [ap2](#ap2)

**Coverage:** see [Evidence](#evidence-verifiable-intent).

### Visa Trusted Agent Protocol (TAP)
<a id="visa-tap"></a>

**Industry, specification.** Specification and sample code published by Visa under Visa Product Terms of October 10, 2025; the product page, read in a browser on 2026-10-14, still says "This product is in the process of development and deployment." (as of 2026-10-14).

**Relevance to commerce.** The merchant specification says "this protocol is applicable to the scenario when the Agent is initially unknown to the Merchant." It defines three signatures: an agent recognition signature based on HTTP Message Signatures (RFC 9421) and aligned with web-bot-auth, a signed consumer or device identity and a signed payment container. The product page is headed "Enhancing Visa Intelligent Commerce to improve merchant visibility.", so the protocol is presented as the merchant-facing recognition part of a wider Visa programme (read in a browser on 2026-10-14); this version has no entry for Visa Intelligent Commerce. A merchant or its site protection provider verifies them; the sample repository calls the protocol "a standardized, cryptographic method for an AI agent to prove its identity and associated authorization directly to merchants."

**Committed next.**
- None declared in the sources read.

**Boundary.** "Merchants are not required to use this proposal and do not necessarily have to be registered with the Payment Schemes".

**Sources.**
- [statement] https://developer.visa.com/capabilities/trusted-agent-protocol (2026-10-14)
- [normative] https://developer.visa.com/capabilities/trusted-agent-protocol/trusted-agent-protocol-specifications (2026-10-14)
- [normative] https://developer.visa.com/capabilities/trusted-agent-protocol/product-terms (2026-10-14)
- [normative] https://github.com/visa/trusted-agent-protocol (2026-10-03)
- [normative] https://github.com/visa/trusted-agent-protocol/blob/main/README.md (2026-10-03)

**Overlaps:** [ietf-webbotauth](#ietf-webbotauth), [mastercard-agent-pay](#mastercard-agent-pay), [verifiable-intent](#verifiable-intent), [aaif-wg-agentic-commerce](#aaif-wg-agentic-commerce)

**Coverage:** see [Evidence](#evidence-visa-tap).

<a id="part-context"></a>
# Part 9: Context entries

### W3C Agent Identity Registry Protocol Community Group
<a id="w3c-agent-identity-registry-cg"></a>

**W3C, venue.** Community group created 2026-04-24; calls every two weeks on Tuesdays at 15:00 UTC; the call of 2026-10-06 had an agenda on the list (message of 2026-10-01) and minutes of 22 September were posted on 2026-10-01; minutes of the call of 2026-10-06 and a later call: not found in the October 2026 list archive (4 messages, the last of 2026-10-02), read 2026-10-13. The W3C group data list one chair. Read 2026-11-02: the group's repository w3c-cg/agent-identity holds agendas and minutes for 2026-09-08, 2026-09-22, 2026-10-06 and 2026-10-20; the minutes of 2026-10-20 (added on 2026-10-29) record a demonstration of credential registration and scope authorization by the chair, no resolutions, and the circulation of a landscape document, and announce the next meeting for 2026-11-17 at 15:00 UTC, postponed for IIW 43 and an agentic workshop. (as of 2026-11-02). Develops specifications to verify an agent's identity, controlling entity and authorization scope; the chair wrote on 2026-08-26 "We'll keep this group focused on the registry and credential layer for now".

**Sources.** [statement] https://api.w3.org/groups/cg/agent-identity (2026-10-02); [statement] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-agent-identity/2026Sep/0017.html (2026-10-03); [statement] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-agent-identity/2026Oct/ (2026-10-13); [statement] https://github.com/w3c-cg/agent-identity/blob/5abd5a677e74ba28d3e889498bc63a9f0e1d367c/meetings/2026/2026-10-20-minutes.md (2026-11-02); [statement] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-agent-identity/2026Aug/0008.html (2026-10-03) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-w3c-agent-identity-registry-cg).

### W3C Agent-Ready Video Community Group
<a id="w3c-agent-ready-video-cg"></a>

**W3C, venue.** Created 2026-10-01; chairs not yet selected: the W3C group data list no chair on 2026-10-13, and the group's list holds one message, the call for participation of 2026-10-01. (as of 2026-10-13). Aims to "make it easier for agents to find, cite and play relevant video on the Web", building on the Agent-Ready Video specification with per-moment rights and signed usage receipts.

**Sources.** [decision] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-new-work/2026Oct/0000.html (2026-10-02) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-w3c-agent-ready-video-cg).

### W3C Agent-to-Web Framework (A2WF) Community Group
<a id="w3c-agent-to-web-framework-cg"></a>

**W3C, venue.** Community group created 2026-03-29; may publish Specifications; one chair is listed on the W3C group data on 2026-10-13, and the list public-a2wf has no October 2026 archive (HTTP 404 on the index). (as of 2026-10-13). Develops technology for website operators to publish machine-readable governance policies for AI agents; examples of agent actions: "fill forms, book appointments, add items to carts, submit orders".

**Sources.** [statement] https://api.w3.org/groups/cg/a2wf (2026-10-02); [normative] https://w3c.github.io/charter-drafts/2026/agenticweb-wg.html (2026-10-02) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-w3c-agent-to-web-framework-cg).

### W3C Browser Testing and Tools Working Group
<a id="w3c-browser-testing-tools-wg"></a>

**W3C, charter.** The Browser Testing and Tools Working Group has a new charter: the W3C charters page of the group lists it as "Chartered under the Patent Policy 2025 from 2026-10-21 until 2028-10-20 (active)", and the charter document gives "Start date 20 October 2026" and "End date 20 October 2028"; the activity page of the group lists a Call for Participation on 2026-10-21, with "Participants had to rejoin under this charter." Chair: David Burns (W3C Invited Experts) (read 2026-10-23). The draft of this charter had been updated by pull request #882 on w3c/charter-drafts, "Update charter-wg-btt.html", merged on 2026-10-07 at 17:24 UTC. (as of 2026-10-23). The scope of the charter "includes protocols and APIs for automation of web browsers, for purposes including automated testing of web applications, and agentic browsing where a user interacts with their browser through an automation API."; the draft change of pull request #882, opened by tantek, said it "Updates to broaden Motivation and Background, Scope sections to include end users and agentic browsing use-cases."

**Sources.** [normative] https://www.w3.org/2026/10/btt-wg-charter.html (2026-10-23); [decision] https://www.w3.org/groups/wg/browser-tools-testing/charters/ (2026-10-23); [decision] https://www.w3.org/groups/wg/browser-tools-testing/activity/ (2026-10-23); [statement] https://www.w3.org/groups/wg/browser-tools-testing/ (2026-10-23); [decision] https://github.com/w3c/charter-drafts/pull/882 (2026-10-08) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-w3c-browser-testing-tools-wg).

### W3C DID Methods Working Group (proposed charter)
<a id="w3c-did-methods-wg"></a>

**W3C, charter.** Proposed charter in Advisory Committee review (2026-09-30); public comments invited through 2026-10-28. (as of 2026-10-13). Mission: "to standardize DID Methods that use Web technologies"; a separate maintenance charter for the existing Decentralized Identifier (DID) Working Group (WG), whose mission includes DID Resolution, went into refinement on 2026-08-10 and was sent to the Advisory Committee on 2026-10-07, with public comments invited through 2026-11-05 (see w3c-did-wg).

**Sources.** [normative] https://www.w3.org/2026/09/proposed-did-methods-wg-charter.html (2026-10-02); [decision] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-new-work/2026Sep/0011.html (2026-10-13); [decision] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-new-work/2026Aug/0003.html (2026-10-13); [decision] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-new-work/2026Oct/0008.html (2026-10-13) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-w3c-did-methods-wg).

### W3C Decentralized Identifier Working Group (proposed charter)
<a id="w3c-did-wg"></a>

**W3C, charter.** Proposed maintenance charter sent to the Advisory Committee on 2026-10-07; W3C invites public comments through 2026-11-05, and the Advisory Committee call for review closes at 23:59 UTC on 2026-11-05. The current charter of the group "is hereby extended until 31 January 2027"; the start and end dates in the proposed charter text are placeholders. (as of 2026-10-09). The mission "is to maintain the specifications produced so far by the Working Group": Decentralized Identifiers and Decentralized Identifier (DID) Resolution; the listed chairs are Will Abramson and Otto Mora. Out of scope are class 4 changes to the DID specification, authentication or authorization protocols, and browser APIs; further notes "will not include DID Method specifications and will not address issues regarding any particular DID Methods". The charter text contains no occurrence of agent, payment, product or commerce (2026-10-08). The call for review says "The Controlled Identifiers (CIDs) recommendation, which is foundational to Decentralized Identifiers, is now also maintained by the DID Working Group." and "The Advisory Committee Representatives of current participants will need to have their organizations re-join the group if the revised charter gets approved."

**Sources.** [normative] https://www.w3.org/2026/10/proposed-did-wg-charter.html (2026-10-08); [decision] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-new-work/2026Oct/0008.html (2026-10-08); [decision] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-did-wg/2026Oct/0002.html (2026-10-09) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-w3c-did-wg).

### W3C Digital Policy Working Group (ODRL, draft charter)
<a id="w3c-digital-policy-wg"></a>

**W3C, charter.** Draft charter in refinement until approx. 2026-11-02 (public-new-work, 2026-09-30); the TPAC session "Proposed Digital Policy Working Group Charter" of 2026-10-28 has draft minutes that record only links, no discussion. Read 2026-11-09: the draft charter of this entry is unchanged since 2026-10-05, and since 2026-11-03 a second draft, "Digital Policy Platform Working Group Charter", sits next to it in the repository w3c/odrl (last changed 2026-11-06). (as of 2026-11-09). Mission: "to develop and maintain the foundational digital policy layer for the digital economy", by extending the two ODRL recommendations.

**Sources.** [decision] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-new-work/2026Sep/0012.html (2026-10-03); [normative] https://w3c.github.io/odrl/w3c-wg-draft-charter-2026/dpwg-draft-charter.html (2026-10-02); [normative] https://github.com/w3c/odrl/blob/2bad2c5/w3c-wg-draft-charter-2026/dppwg-draft-charter.html (2026-11-09); [decision] https://www.w3.org/2026/10/28-digital-policy-wg-minutes.html (2026-11-09) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-w3c-digital-policy-wg).

### W3C Web Payment Security Interest Group
<a id="w3c-web-payment-security-ig"></a>

**W3C, venue.** Chartered 2025-04-01 to 2027-03-31 (shortname securepay); the draft Agentic Web charter lists the IG for "agentic payments". At TPAC the IG met on 2026-10-26, with agenda items on agentic payment updates from a FIDO technical working group and on the EMVCo Agentic Payments Framework, and on 2026-10-27, jointly with other groups; no resolutions are recorded in the draft minutes (read 2026-11-09). On 2026-11-02 WebKit published an explainer and "a very early and unofficial draft" of a payment method, digital-credential-payment, that uses the Digital Credentials API inside Payment Request (WebKit/explainers PR #160, merged the same day), after announcing it at the Web Payments WG meeting of 2026-10-29; it is not a W3C document. (as of 2026-11-09). Mission: "enhance the security and interoperability of various Web payments technologies"; a W3C staff member expected the e-commerce workshop to identify future workstreams for this group (webai-roadmap#45, 2026-06-11).

**Sources.** [normative] https://www.w3.org/securepay/charter-2025.html (2026-10-02); [statement] https://github.com/w3c/webai-roadmap/issues/45 (2026-10-03); [decision] https://www.w3.org/2026/10/26-wpsig-minutes.html (2026-11-09); [decision] https://www.w3.org/2026/10/27-wpsig-minutes.html (2026-11-09); [third party] https://github.com/WebKit/explainers/pull/160 (2026-11-09); [statement] https://www.w3.org/2026/10/29-wpwg-minutes.html (2026-11-09) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-w3c-web-payment-security-ig).

### WebMCP (Draft Community Group Report, Web Machine Learning CG)
<a id="w3c-webmcp-cg-report"></a>

**W3C, specification.** Draft Community Group Report, dated 9 October 2026 on the page read on 2026-10-13 (2026-09-30 on 2026-10-03), no numbered version; on 2026-10-08 PR #338, "Remove declarative API from spec", was merged (the specification now focuses on the imperative registerTool() API); the draft Agentic Web Working Group (WG) charter lists WebMCP as its normative deliverable. At TPAC, the Web Machine Learning CG meeting of 2026-10-27 recorded resolutions (draft minutes, read 2026-11-09), among them: to explore making the API "a more generic communication tool"; agreement on the ModelContextClient API shape (issue #227); "Declarative WebMCP work continues in the explainer form within this repo, advancement to spec stage will be considered once implementation experience is available"; and that general "Web Security and Privacy Guidance for Agent Implementers" belongs in a group chartered for that work. (as of 2026-11-09). Draft: "The WebMCP API enables web applications to provide JavaScript-based tools to AI agents."; an editor wrote on 2026-09-25 that identity and delegation proposals "are beyond the scope of the simple WebMCP API".

**Sources.** [normative] https://webmachinelearning.github.io/webmcp/ (2026-10-02); [third party] https://github.com/webmachinelearning/webmcp/issues/257 (2026-10-03); [decision] https://github.com/webmachinelearning/webmcp/pull/338 (2026-10-13); [decision] https://www.w3.org/2026/10/27-webmachinelearning-minutes.html (2026-11-09); [third party] https://github.com/WebKit/standards-positions/issues/670 (2026-10-03) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-w3c-webmcp-cg-report).

### DNS for AI Discovery (DNS-AID, draft-mozleywilliams-dnsop-dnsaid)
<a id="ietf-dnsaid"></a>

**IETF, work-item.** Individual Internet-Draft -02 (2026-05-27), "(No stream defined)", not adopted by a Working Group (WG); expires 2026-11-28. Related individual proposals, none adopted by a WG (datatracker, 2026-10-13): draft-ihsanullah-dnsid-01 (2026-06-29), draft-ferro-dnsop-apertoid-02 (2026-08-12), draft-morrison-mcp-dns-discovery-08 (2026-10-09; revision -09 posted 2026-10-22, datatracker, read 2026-11-02) and draft-ranjbar-dane-did-01 (2026-08-01), and a fifth, draft-ranjbar-dane-anchored-identity-00 (2026-07-21; datatracker, 2026-10-15); DNS-AID is not among them. The IETF Secretariat announced on 2026-10-09 (15:20 UTC) a non-WG list, dns-underscore-keys (area INT), whose purpose is "To compare existing proposals for publishing keys, or references to them, under underscore labels in DNS" and names those four drafts; its public archive held three messages on 2026-10-13. The first post, by an author of one of the four drafts (2026-10-10), compares them on five axes: who the record is about, where the key sits, what makes the record trustworthy, revocation, and continuity of holding after a domain changes hands; it leaves open the enumeration of names in a zone, record size, and TXT against a new RRTYPE. On 2026-10-12 an author of another of the four wrote that "A 3 1 1 TLSA with SHA-256 is 35 bytes of RDATA" and "The full signed answer with the DO bit set is 271 bytes", measured on one live record (figures declared by the source, not reproduced here). The draft texts were not read for this entry; their content is as the list messages describe it. (as of 2026-10-13). The abstract says the document "standardizes an approach for publishing AI agents in the Domain Name System (DNS) so that other agents can discover them". The draft publishes AI agents in DNS; Section 5.6 reserves SvcParamKeys for "commercial signaling such as cost-per-unit-of-input, unit-of-input definitions, billing tiers, or rate-limit hints".

**Sources.** [third party] https://github.com/danubetech/did-method-dns/issues/12 (2026-10-15); [third party] https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-credentials/2026Jul/0041.html (2026-10-19); [third party] https://www.ietf.org/archive/id/draft-mozleywilliams-dnsop-dnsaid-02.txt (2026-10-02); [decision] https://datatracker.ietf.org/wg/dnsop/documents/ (2026-10-02); [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ihsanullah-dnsid/ (2026-10-13); [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ferro-dnsop-apertoid/ (2026-10-13); [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-morrison-mcp-dns-discovery/ (2026-11-02); [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ranjbar-dane-did/ (2026-10-13); [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ranjbar-dane-anchored-identity/ (2026-10-15); [third party] https://mailarchive.ietf.org/arch/msg/dns-underscore-keys/Atm6JcPnAlMDGYTfUwksFWi9KO4/ (2026-10-13); [third party] https://mailarchive.ietf.org/arch/msg/dns-underscore-keys/IZ2sMGeSEHr-A16iL6VCwtQWQeY/ (2026-10-13); [third party] https://mailarchive.ietf.org/arch/msg/dns-underscore-keys/9lSuPLAWz8e5aFdcwhYr4cutWYI/ (2026-10-13); [third party] https://github.com/frmoretto/agentic-standards-map/issues/3 (2026-10-19); [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-mozleywilliams-dnsop-dnsaid-02 (2026-10-05) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-ietf-dnsaid).

### IETF WIMSE, AI Identity Management System (draft-ietf-wimse-aims)
<a id="ietf-wimse-aims"></a>

**IETF, work-item.** Working Group (WG) document draft-ietf-wimse-aims-00 (2026-09-15), Informational; adopted 2026-09-09, expires 2027-03-19; replaces draft-klrc-aiagent-auth. The datatracker record of 2026-10-13 gives revision 00 (time 2026-09-15 14:40 UTC) and no later revision. On 2026-11-02 the datatracker still gives revision 00. The individual draft draft-zambo-aer1 (AER-1, revision -26 posted 2026-10-23; datatracker, read 2026-11-02) specifies "a small vocabulary for recording one AI agent tool call as a portable, independently checkable execution receipt"; it is not a WG document, and its author proposed it in issue #19 of this map's repository. Its author publishes a conformance corpus at zambo.dev/aer1/corpus.json (format aer1-evaluated-corpus/1, kit 1.5.7, referring to draft-zambo-aer1-26, 143 vectors and 146 checks, with SHA-256 digests in the index; read 2026-11-03 and again 2026-11-09, unchanged); the index shows that the corpus exists, not that the checks pass, and its scripts were not run. A companion individual draft, draft-zambo-aer1-wimse-mapping, is at revision -02 of 2026-11-01 (datatracker, read 2026-11-09; text not read). The individual draft draft-hulea-wimse-agent-mandate-00 (2026-10-24, not a WG document) cites draft-ietf-wimse-aims and defines how a relying party in one administrative domain validates the mandate under which an agent acts for a principal in another domain, adding evidence that the delegator was entitled to grant it, kept separate from the grant itself; its examples include an agent initiating a payment at a bank (text read 2026-11-02; revision -01 of 2026-11-01 is on the datatracker, read 2026-11-09, and its text was not read for this map). (as of 2026-11-09). The draft "proposes best practices for authentication and authorization of AI agent interactions"; commerce, merchant, seller or purchase text: not found in -00, 2026-10-02. ACP and AP2 appear only in the references.

**Sources.** [normative] https://www.ietf.org/archive/id/draft-ietf-wimse-aims-00.txt (2026-10-02); [decision] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-wimse-aims/ (2026-10-02); [decision] https://datatracker.ietf.org/meeting/127/agenda.json (2026-10-26); [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-zambo-aer1/ (2026-11-02); [third party] https://www.ietf.org/archive/id/draft-hulea-wimse-agent-mandate-00.txt (2026-11-02); [third party] https://zambo.dev/aer1/corpus.json (2026-11-09); [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-zambo-aer1-wimse-mapping/ (2026-11-09); [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-hulea-wimse-agent-mandate/ (2026-11-09) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-ietf-wimse-aims).

### Payment HTTP Authentication Scheme (draft-httpauth-payment, Tempo Labs and Stripe)
<a id="ietf-httpauth-payment"></a>

**IETF, specification.** Individual draft-httpauth-payment-01 (2026-09-09), Standards Track intended, no Working Group (WG), expires 2027-03-13; authors from Tempo Labs and Stripe. The datatracker record of 2026-10-13 gives revision 01 (time 2026-09-09 16:08 UTC) and no later revision. (as of 2026-10-14). On receipts, -01 reads "Servers SHOULD include a Payment-Receipt header on successful responses"; the receipt table has four fields (status, method, timestamp, reference) and no signature field.

**Sources.** [decision] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-httpauth-payment/ (2026-10-03); [third party] https://www.ietf.org/archive/id/draft-httpauth-payment-01.txt (2026-10-03); [statement] https://docs.stripe.com/payments/machine/mpp (2026-10-14) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-ietf-httpauth-payment).

### GS1 Digital Link Standard, URI Syntax 1.7.0
<a id="gs1-digital-link"></a>

**GS1, specification.** Release 1.7.0 ratified August 2026; the archive on ref.gs1.org lists "Published 7 August 2026". (as of 2026-10-13). The syntax "distinguishes between primary identification keys that express simple GS1 identifiers, such as GTIN and GLN" and key qualifiers and attributes; a GS1 Digital Link URI "is a particular form of URL". The standard says applications "SHALL NOT assume" that a URL in this syntax points to a resolver, and names a Resolver Description File as one test (section 2).

**Sources.** [normative] https://ref.gs1.org/standards/digital-link/uri-syntax/ (2026-11-09); [decision] https://ref.gs1.org/standards/digital-link/uri-syntax/archive (2026-11-09) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-gs1-digital-link).

### GS1 EPCIS and Core Business Vocabulary (CBV)
<a id="gs1-epcis"></a>

**GS1, specification.** The EPCIS archive on ref.gs1.org gives 2.0.1 as the latest version, published 2025-07-01, while the PDF served at /standards/epcis/2.0.1/ prints "Release 2.0, Ratified, Jun 2022" and does not contain the string 2.0.1. The archive pairs EPCIS 2.0.1 with the Core Business Vocabulary (CBV) 2.0.0, published 2022-06-22. (as of 2026-11-09). EPCIS data consist of "visibility events", each the record of a business process step acting on objects such as trade items (products), and implementations SHALL understand queries in both EPC URN and GS1 Digital Link URI syntax (sections 1, 7). The GS1 Web Vocabulary defines the link type gs1:epcis, "A link to an EPCIS repository of visibility event data.", and the GS1-Conformant Resolver 1.2.1 names it in an example of traceability information (section 2.5.10).

**Sources.** [normative] https://ref.gs1.org/standards/epcis/2.0.1/ (2026-11-09); [decision] https://ref.gs1.org/standards/epcis/archive (2026-11-09); [normative] https://ref.gs1.org/standards/epcis/artefacts (2026-11-09); [normative] https://ref.gs1.org/standards/cbv/2.0.0/ (2026-11-09); [decision] https://ref.gs1.org/standards/cbv/archive (2026-11-09); [normative] https://ref.gs1.org/voc/data/gs1Voc.jsonld (2026-11-09); [normative] https://ref.gs1.org/standards/resolver/ (2026-11-09) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-gs1-epcis).

### GS1 Web Vocabulary
<a id="gs1-web-vocabulary"></a>

**GS1, specification.** Version 1.18: the ontology carries owl:versionInfo "1.18" and dct:modified 2026-06-01, and the archive on ref.gs1.org lists version 1.18.0 published 2026-06-01. A ratification label with a date for 1.18 is not found in the pages read. The change log, read 2026-11-09, gives no dates, and its newest entry is 1.17.0, so it does not yet list 1.18. (as of 2026-11-09). The vocabulary "is designed as an extension to schema.org" and defines parties and a product offer; gs1:Product, gs1:Offer and gs1:Organization carry skos:exactMatch mappings to the schema.org terms of the same name, and gs1:gtin to schema:gtin14. It also holds the link types used in GS1 Digital Link, under the namespace https://ref.gs1.org/voc/, with data in JSON-LD and Turtle and JSON-LD context files that the landing page labels "provisional".

**Sources.** [normative] https://ref.gs1.org/voc/ (2026-11-09); [normative] https://ref.gs1.org/voc/data/gs1Voc.jsonld (2026-11-09); [decision] https://ref.gs1.org/voc/data/archive (2026-11-09); [decision] https://ref.gs1.org/voc/data/changeLog.json (2026-11-09) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-gs1-web-vocabulary).

### UN Global Trust Register, namespace urn:gtr
<a id="un-gtr-urn"></a>

**UN/CEFACT, work-item.** Namespace "IANA registration pending" per the UN Transparency Protocol (UNTP) specification. Not found in the Internet Assigned Numbers Authority (IANA) Uniform Resource Name (URN) namespaces registry (updated 2026-10-12, read 2026-10-13): a record named gtr. (as of 2026-10-13). The specification says "Either register your own scheme with IANA or use the UN global trust register gtr URN namespace (IANA registration pending)", pattern urn:gtr:{identifier-scheme}:{identifier-value}.

**Sources.** [normative] https://untp.unece.org/docs/specification/IdentityResolver/ (2026-10-02); [decision] https://www.iana.org/assignments/urn-namespaces/urn-namespaces.xml (2026-10-13) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-un-gtr-urn).

<a id="ieee-p3931-addr"></a>
