### AAIF Identity and Trust group, issue #13 and the agentrust-io integrations (TRACE)
<a id="aaif-identity-trust"></a>

**AAIF, work-item.** Issue #13 of aaif/wg-identity-and-trust compares how MintID and Proofable record authority at the point where an agent action is dispatched; it was open with 18 comments on 2026-10-19, the last of that day. The shared runner proposed for it, pull request #288 of agentrust-io/integrations (head 74fc43c), was merged on 2026-10-13 at 18:00 UTC (merge commit 5f9b48e) by the project lead of TRACE, who had approved it on 2026-10-12, and its tracking issue #287 was closed the same minute. TRACE, the format those integrations cite, is a specification (v0.2, Developer Preview) with a released reference package (agentrust-trace 0.11.0, 2026-09-25). The independent run: on issue #13, a participant from Bitgrit ("Role: independent operator; Bitgrit submits no implementation. Disclosure: Bitgrit develops DID/VC identity infrastructure.") wrote on 2026-10-14 that "Bitgrit is the executing partner for the independent reproduction", with an active start date of 2026-10-13, and on 2026-10-18 that Round 1 was set to the baseline frozen on 2026-10-13 "to meet the October 30 deadline". On 2026-10-19 the same participant posted an "Independent Operator Round 1 Final Comparison Record" for four cases, all with the implementation "MintID / agent-evidence-observer", run against the MintID tag public-v2026-10-17.1; the record states a DENY dispatch decision in each of the four cases. These results are the operator's own statement; the archive the record links was not downloaded and nothing in it was checked here. The group's Reference Architecture sub-group has a working draft, "Agent Identity Primitives" (Draft, version 0.1, dated 2026-08-27), in a Google Doc readable without a login (read 2026-10-13 and 2026-10-19). Its scope clause reads "The following are out of scope: authorization models (what an agent is permitted to do), evidence, reputation, and security controls or threat modeling.", and its section 3.4 (Delegation) reads "This section is under active discussion." Comments in the document propose changes that are not yet in the text; they are not quoted here. Commerce, merchant, seller and purchase: not found in the document text (2026-10-19). Messages of the group's list after message 48: not read (the archive requires JavaScript and its rules ask for a licence for automated AI access). (as of 2026-10-19). The group's README says "This working group meets every Thursday, alternating between 6:30AM and 9:00AM USA Pacific Time." and points to a public mailing list on lists.aaif.io; the archive of that list was not read, and six messages of it (numbers 40 and 44 to 48, 2026-10-06 to 2026-10-08) were read by the author on 2026-10-09. Proofable (proofable/docs): an earlier evidence packet (commits 3a45f02 to 4ceb58b, 2026-10-06 to 2026-10-08) had manifest digests computed over CRLF bytes, as its author stated on 2026-10-07; the packet frozen at commit 9851059 (2026-10-08) matches its SHA256SUMS (recomputed on 2026-10-13), and its author lists the limit "Author-operated (SELF); no independent witness." MintID publishes tags on GitLab (17, from public-v2026-10-05.5 to public-v2026-10-18.1, read 2026-10-19) and a CHANGELOG.md at the root of the tagged tree, present from public-v2026-10-06, which opens "What changed for users of MintID in each public release"; no tag links to a release object. List messages: a participant from MintID wrote on 2026-10-08 that "From public-v2026-10-08.1 a 5xx is recorded as a service interruption, never as a decision, and the run completes"; a participant from ALIGN, an organisation preparing an independent run, wrote that ALIGN "will pin public-v2026-10-08.1 or later" and "We will confirm the start date on October 15."; a participant who submitted the runner wrote on 2026-10-08 that results are recorded "as operator-reported until independently reproduced". The runner (issue #287, pull request #288) says "This is a shared evidence-appraisal runner, not a comparative certification." and "No Verified tier or conformance level is requested."; the test counts given at different heads (27 in the pull request, 28, 34) are declared by the sources and not reconciled here. The README of agentrust-io/integrations defines two tiers, Community ("We do not run your code") and Verified, and says the tier "is set by maintainers, never self-declared". TRACE (agentrust-io/trace-spec): "Developer Preview. Launched at Confidential Computing Summit, 23 June 2026. Spec v0.2 is current."; the specification text is under the Community Specification License 1.0 and the code under Apache 2.0; the project lead listed in MAINTAINERS.md is Imran Siddique (AgenTrust-io).

**Sources.** [third party] https://github.com/aaif/wg-identity-and-trust/issues/13 (2026-10-19); [statement] https://github.com/aaif/wg-identity-and-trust/blob/main/README.md (2026-10-09); [third party] https://github.com/agentrust-io/integrations/issues/286 (2026-10-09); ### AAIF structure, Technical Committee and working groups
<a id="aaif-structure"></a>

**AAIF, venue.** AAIF has a Governing Board, a Technical Committee and working groups, each with a repository; Agentic Commerce has its own entry. Hosted projects listed on aaif.io/projects (read 2026-11-02): Agent Router, Agent Skills, Agent2Agent, AGENTS.md, agentgateway, Goose, Model Context Protocol, Open Responses, OpenEnv and OpenSandbox, unchanged since 2026-10-23. AAIF announced on 2026-10-22 "four new projects: Agent Skills, contributed by Anthropic; Open Responses, contributed by OpenAI; OpenEnv, contributed by Hugging Face; and OpenSandbox, contributed by Alibaba", and wrote "The additions bring AAIF to 10 hosted projects in its first year, up from its three launch projects in December 2025." AAIF announced on 2026-10-22 that it "has reached 305 members in under a year since its launch in December 2025" (read 2026-11-02). (as of 2026-11-02). aaif.io describes AAIF as "a neutral, open foundation under the Linux Foundation". Its Identity and Trust group aims at standards so "agents can be discovered across platforms, prove who they are, who they act for". Taxonomy and Landscape Workstream (repository ws-taxonomy-landscape, read on 2026-10-09 at commit 6ac90f5 of main): its charter lists the deliverables "Shared Taxonomy Explorer (v1.0)" with the date 2026-08-15 and "Ecosystem Architecture Map (v1.0)", `landscape.yml` plus a web application, with the date 2026-10-15; these are dates of a charter table, not a commitment of the workstream. On 2026-10-13 a portal titled "Taxonomy and Landscape Portal" is published at aaif.github.io/ws-taxonomy-landscape (page marked updated 2026-10-11, 8:13 pm) with links to a Shared Taxonomy Explorer and an Ecosystem Landscape Map; neither is called v1.0 on the page, the repository has no release (0) and no tag read, its commits to 2026-10-12 define taxonomy terms, and the date 2026-10-15 of the Ecosystem Architecture Map has passed with no release and no tag in the repository (read 2026-11-02). A Landscape is published at landscape.aaif.io, linked as "Landscape" from the aaif.io menu (read 2026-11-02): a Members category (174 entries in four tiers) and an "Agentic AI Momentum Watchlist" of 178 projects in five subcategories, whose Protocols & Interfaces subcategory lists, among others, the Universal Commerce Protocol (UCP), the Agentic Commerce Protocol (ACP), the Agent Payments Protocol (AP2), the Agent2Agent (A2A) Protocol, Agentic Resource Discovery (ARD), the Machine Payments Protocol (MPP), WebMCP and x402. The workstream README still carries the sentence on the Landscape quoted below (read 2026-11-02). Its README says "The Landscape remains on the future roadmap, but its contribution and review process has not yet been accepted." and that "pull requests that propose Landscape changes will be closed"; the text was added by PR #61 ("docs: clarify current taxonomy-only contribution scope", merged 2026-09-16). PR #83 ("Clarify taxonomy contribution process", merged 2026-10-07) rewrote CONTRIBUTING.md: a term is raised in a Working Group, a delegate adds it to the taxonomy spreadsheet, the weekly workstream meeting votes KEEP, CULL or DEBATE, and only then does a delegate open a pull request, which needs approval from delegates of two different Working Groups before it is merged and the term is marked FINALIZED; the guide adds "A pull request for a term that has not received a KEEP vote will be closed."

**Sources.** [statement] https://aaif.io/projects (2026-11-02); [decision] https://aaif.io/news/agentic-ai-foundation-announces-four-new-projects (2026-10-23); [statement] https://aaif.io/about (2026-10-03); [normative] https://github.com/aaif/wg-identity-and-trust/blob/main/charter.md (2026-10-03); [normative] https://github.com/aaif/ws-taxonomy-landscape/blob/main/charter/charter.md (2026-10-09); [normative] https://github.com/aaif/ws-taxonomy-landscape/blob/main/README.md (2026-11-02); [normative] https://github.com/aaif/ws-taxonomy-landscape/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md (2026-10-09); [decision] https://github.com/aaif/ws-taxonomy-landscape/pull/61 (2026-10-09); [decision] https://github.com/aaif/ws-taxonomy-landscape/pull/83 (2026-10-09); [statement] https://aaif.github.io/ws-taxonomy-landscape/ (2026-10-13); [statement] https://home.mlops.community/public/events/accuracy-and-reliability-of-ai-agents-virtual-summit-77nje0s530 (2026-10-13); [statement] https://aaif.io/news/agentic-ai-foundation-surpasses-300-members (2026-11-02); [statement] https://landscape.aaif.io/ (2026-11-02); [third party] https://github.com/frmoretto/agentic-standards-map/issues/13 (2026-10-19) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-aaif-structure).

### Agent2Agent (A2A) protocol, core specification
<a id="a2a-core"></a>

**AAIF, specification.** Hosted project of the Agentic AI Foundation (AAIF): AAIF announced on 2026-08-17 that A2A "is joining the Agentic AI Foundation (AAIF) as a hosted project", and the A2A blog of 2026-08-27 says it "has officially been accepted as a Growth Stage project"; latest release v1.0.1, 2026-05-28. Technical Steering Committee of nine company seats since 2026-10-20 (GOVERNANCE.md, commit 98279d6, which added Bloomberg; read 2026-11-09): Google, Microsoft, Cisco, AWS, Salesforce, ServiceNow, SAP, IBM, Bloomberg. (as of 2026-11-09). The AgentCard is defined in specification/a2a.proto as "A self-describing manifest for an agent."; how cards are found is in a2a-discovery. PR #2081, merged on 2026-07-30, added section 7.6.4 to the specification: "Agents MUST NOT treat the TASK_STATE_AUTH_REQUIRED state transition, by itself, as authorization for any particular operation."; the PR description calls the state "a coordination signal, not an authorization grant".

**Sources.** [decision] https://aaif.io/blog/a2a-joins-aaif (2026-10-19); [statement] https://a2a-protocol.org/latest/blog/2026/08/27/a-new-chapter-for-a2a-joining-the-agentic-ai-foundation/ (2026-10-19); [statement] https://aaif.io/projects (2026-10-19); [normative] https://github.com/a2aproject/A2A/blob/main/specification/a2a.proto (2026-10-03); [normative] https://github.com/a2aproject/A2A/blob/main/GOVERNANCE.md (2026-11-09); [decision] https://www.linuxfoundation.org/press/linux-foundation-launches-the-agent2agent-protocol-project-to-enable-secure-intelligent-communication-between-ai-agents (2026-10-03); [normative] https://github.com/a2aproject/A2A/blob/main/docs/specification.md (2026-10-08); [third party] https://github.com/a2aproject/A2A/pull/2081 (2026-10-08); [third party] https://github.com/frmoretto/agentic-standards-map/issues/7 (2026-10-19); [statement] https://github.com/a2aproject/A2A/blob/main/README.md (2026-10-19); [normative] https://www.iana.org/assignments/well-known-uris/well-known-uris.xml (2026-10-05); [third party] https://github.com/frmoretto/agentic-standards-map/issues/5 (2026-10-16) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-a2a-core).

### Model Context Protocol (MCP), core specification
<a id="mcp-core"></a>

**AAIF, specification.** Specification version 2026-07-28 (the latest release is 2026-07-28; the specification directory holds 2025-03-26, 2025-06-18, 2025-11-25, 2026-07-28 and a draft; no later release, read 2026-10-13); the blog post of 2025-12-09 says "Anthropic is donating MCP to the Agentic AI Foundation". (as of 2026-10-13). The specification describes MCP as "an open protocol that enables seamless integration between LLM applications and external data sources and tools"; discovery is in mcp-server-card and lf-ai-catalog.

### Accord Project (agreement layer for agentic commerce)
<a id="accord-project"></a>

**Linux Foundation, specification.** Open-source smart legal contract stack of Accord Project, a Series of LF Projects, LLC; its documentation page "Agentic Commerce" (no date on the page, last changed 2026-07-07 in the repository history, read 2026-10-15) describes it as the agreement layer beneath authorization and checkout protocols. The Concerto model of x402 presented on 2026-07-22 is pull request #194 of accordproject/models, open on 2026-10-15 (last update 2026-08-05). (as of 2026-10-15). The documentation page says "Accord Project is the agreement layer" and calls its three integration patterns (an authorization credential binding a contract by hash, Concerto as schema language for a constraint registry, contract logic for terms "authorization can't enforce") "illustrative", describing "technical fit, not partnerships or endorsements" with AP2, UCP, ACP and Verifiable Intent. Two prototypes by individual members of the project's Technical Steering Committee (TSC), outside the project's repositories and without licence or release, apply them with stubbed or simulated settlement: mttrbrts/agentic-obligations (last commit 2026-05-20) and The-Building-Blocks/accord-x402-contract-server (last commit 2026-07-30). They bind a contract reference in AP2 mandates and in x402 payment requirements, and the second says the x402 payment signature "does not sign the Accord `agreementHash`".

**Sources.** [statement] https://docs.accordproject.org/docs/accordproject-agentic-commerce (2026-10-15); [statement] https://accordproject.org/news/accord-project-tech-working-group-q3-2026-in-review/ (2026-10-15); [statement] https://github.com/accordproject/technical-steering-committee/blob/main/README.md (2026-10-15); [third party] https://github.com/accordproject/models/pull/194 (2026-10-15); [statement] https://github.com/accordproject/apap/blob/main/server/README.md (2026-10-15); [third party] https://github.com/mttrbrts/agentic-obligations (2026-10-15); [third party] https://github.com/The-Building-Blocks/accord-x402-contract-server (2026-10-15); [third party] https://github.com/frmoretto/agentic-standards-map/issues/1 (2026-10-16) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-accord-project).

### DIF Trusted AI Agents Working Group
<a id="dif-trusted-ai-agents"></a>

The group defines "an opinionated, interoperable stack to enable trustworthy, privacy-preserving, and secure AI agents"; KYA-OS (donated by Vouched, March 2026) covers agent identity and delegation with DIDs and VCs.

### TRACE (Trust, Runtime Attestation and Compliance Evidence)
<a id="trace"></a>

**Linux Foundation, specification.** Specification version 0.2, described in its text as "0.2: Draft" with status "RFC: Request for Comments"; the repository README calls it a Developer Preview launched on 23 June 2026, and the Python package agentrust-trace 0.11.0 was released on 2026-09-25. The README states that the specification is hosted at the Linux Foundation as "TRACE Specification, a Series of LF Projects, LLC", and a Linux Foundation press release of 2026-08-25 announces the contribution of TRACE from OPAQUE. Reading of 2026-10-13: the README of the repository now says "a proposal to bring it into the Agentic AI Foundation as a Sandbox project is in progress" and points to aaif/project-proposals issue #42, which is open with the label Under Review, 8 comments and a last update on 2026-10-02; the specification stays at v0.2 ("Spec v0.2 is current"), main is at commit 8562b09 (2026-10-13, 00:33 UTC), and the latest release objects are conformance-v0.6.2 (2026-10-03, the conformance test package) and v0.11.0 (2026-09-25, the Python package agentrust-trace 0.11.0, numbered apart from the specification). The folder spec/ also holds a draft named mcp-profile-v0.3-draft.md, not read. Reading of 2026-10-21: the README still says "Spec v0.2 is current" and still describes the AAIF Sandbox proposal as "in progress"; issue #42 is open, labelled Under Review, with 9 comments and a last update on 2026-10-15. In its last comment (2026-10-15) the filer, the project lead of TRACE, writes that the contributing entity is now "TRACE Specification, a Series of LF Projects, LLC" and that "OPAQUE Systems wound down on 12 October"; he also repeats a question of 2026-09-21 about a Technical Committee sponsor, and the issue shows no reply to it. These statements are the filer's own; no AAIF decision was read. MAINTAINERS.md now lists the Project Lead, two repository maintainers and two reviewers (since 2026-10-15); the two OPAQUE spec editors read on 2026-10-09 are no longer listed. (as of 2026-10-21). The specification says "This is the rulebook for TRACE: it defines the Trust Record (a signed receipt describing one AI agent run), how records are anchored in a" The README describes TRACE as "an open format for a signed receipt of an AI agent run: what ran, where, under which rules, on what kind of data, and which" and states its limit: "A v0.2 signature proves who made a record and that it has not changed. It does not prove the hardware claims inside it". The specification text is under the Community Specification License 1.0 and the code under Apache 2.0 (LICENSE and README); GitHub's repository metadata reports the licence as NOASSERTION. MAINTAINERS.md lists as Project Lead Imran Siddique (AgenTrust-io), as spec editors Rishabh Poddar and Aaron Fulkerson (OPAQUE Systems), and two repository maintainers listed in the same file. The Linux Foundation press release says "The Linux Foundation will provide vendor-neutral governance for TRACE to support sustainability and long-term growth." In the AAIF Identity and Trust work TRACE is the format cited by the shared runner of agentrust-io/integrations, which validates "reference shapes" of the released package agentrust-trace 0.11.0, a reference package, not a version of the specification, which is v0.2 (see aaif-identity-trust).

**Sources.** [statement] https://github.com/agentrust-io/trace-spec/blob/main/README.md (2026-10-21); [statement] https://github.com/aaif/project-proposals/issues/42 (2026-10-21); [normative] https://github.com/agentrust-io/trace-spec/blob/main/spec/trace-v0.2.md (2026-10-09); [statement] https://github.com/agentrust-io/trace-spec/blob/main/MAINTAINERS.md (2026-10-21); [normative] https://github.com/agentrust-io/trace-spec/blob/main/LICENSE (2026-10-09); [decision] https://github.com/agentrust-io/trace-spec/releases/tag/v0.11.0 (2026-10-26); [statement] https://www.linuxfoundation.org/press/linux-foundation-welcomes-trace-to-advance-verifiable-runtime-evidence-for-ai-workloads (2026-10-09) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-trace).

### EMILIA Protocol (individual drafts and open-source gate)
<a id="emilia-protocol"></a>

**Independent, work-item.** Two individual Internet-Drafts by Iman Schrock (EMILIA Protocol, Inc.) were current on 2026-10-08: draft-schrock-ep-architecture-03 (2026-09-06, Informational) and draft-schrock-action-evidence-boundary-07 (2026-09-25, Informational). The Apache-2.0 repository emiliaprotocol/emilia-protocol was created on 2026-03-13. (as of 2026-10-08). The architecture draft "composes the Canonical Action Identifier (CAID), Authorization Evidence Chain (AEC), and Action Evidence Boundary (AEB)" and says "A valid signature, a current credential, a satisfied evidence requirement, and an observed effect remain different facts." The README says "EMILIA Gate checks authority before a protected tool runs"; its example MCP payment server "refuses a call without approval, binds approval to the amount, currency, vendor, and destination", and the README calls the example "a local demonstration" with "a mock payment tool". The boundary draft refers to AP2 repeatedly (counts in the evidence). On the AUDIT list its author and Sumit Ahuja are writing an expected-record draft that cites the boundary draft (see ietf-audit); on the agentproto list the author pointed on 2026-10-03 to the repository's response-loss example. AAIF Identity and Trust issue #13 compares MintID and Proofable; EMILIA does not appear in the comments of that issue read on 2026-10-08.

**Sources.** [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-schrock-ep-architecture/ (2026-10-08); [third party] https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-schrock-action-evidence-boundary/ (2026-10-08); [third party] https://github.com/emiliaprotocol/emilia-protocol (2026-10-08); [third party] https://mailarchive.ietf.org/arch/msg/agentproto/_-7ahBTxuzTScC_1yCTarPXsbzU/ (2026-10-08); [third party] https://github.com/aaif/wg-identity-and-trust/issues/13 (2026-10-08) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-emilia-protocol).

### OpenID Foundation AuthZEN Working Group (agent authorization drafts AARP and COAZ)
<a id="oidf-authzen-agents"></a>

**Independent, work-item.** AARP and COAZ adopted as Working Group Drafts on 2026-06-15; to be refined through community review and interoperability testing. (as of 2026-10-13). AARP covers prerequisites such as approvals, consent or attestations before a policy decision; COAZ, the profile for MCP tool authorization, maps information models into the Subject-Action-Resource-Context structure. The OpenID Foundation published a whitepaper, "Identity Management for Agentic AI" (OpenID Foundation page 2025-10-07, arXiv 2510.25819 v1 2025-10-29), on authentication and authorization for AI agents; it "outlines the resources already available for securing today's agents and presents a strategic agenda". The whitepaper names Web Bot Auth, one IETF proposal described as a "passport for agents", and the Agent Payments Protocol (AP2); it does not name UCP, ACP, x402 or TAP (text search, counts in the evidence).

**Sources.** [third party] https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.25819 (2026-10-06); [third party] https://openid.net/new-whitepaper-tackles-ai-agent-identity-challenges/ (2026-10-06); [decision] https://openid.net/openid-foundation-advances-authorization-for-the-agent-era-with-new-authzen-working-group-drafts/ (2026-10-13); [statement] https://openid.net/wg/ (2026-10-02) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-oidf-authzen-agents).

### FIDO Alliance agentic authentication and payments work
<a id="fido-agentic-standards"></a>

**Industry, work-item.** Announced 2026-04-28: an Agentic Authentication Technical Working Group, and work in the Payments Technical Working Group starting from Google (AP2) and Mastercard (Verifiable Intent) contributions. (as of 2026-10-14). The release says the Payments Technical Working Group is "chaired by members from Mastercard and Visa"; it says contributed; Google's announcement of the same day says "We're donating Agent Payments Protocol to the FIDO Alliance", and the AP2 repository says its core specification has been donated to FIDO. For the Agentic Authentication Technical Working Group the release says it is "chaired by members from CVS Health, Google and OpenAI and vice-chaired by members from Amazon, Google and Okta" (read in a browser on 2026-10-14); it names companies, not persons.

**Sources.** [decision] https://blog.google/products-and-platforms/platforms/google-pay/agent-payments-protocol-fido-alliance/ (2026-10-19); [decision] https://fidoalliance.org/fido-alliance-to-develop-standards-for-trusted-ai-agent-interactions/ (2026-10-14); [normative] https://github.com/google-agentic-commerce/AP2/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md (2026-10-03); [third party] https://github.com/frmoretto/agentic-standards-map/issues/12 (2026-10-19) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-fido-agentic-standards).

### IAB Tech Lab AAMP 3.0 and OpenProposal (agentic advertising, draft)
<a id="iab-tech-lab-aamp"></a>

**Industry, work-item.** IAB Tech Lab announced AAMP 3.0, "the next major release of its Agentic Advertising Management Protocols framework", on 2026-09-22, with the specification OpenProposal, whose public comment period ran "until October 22, 2026". The README of IABTechLab/OpenProposal says "Nothing here is a ratified IAB Tech Lab standard.", and the specification at tag v3.0-draft-1 (2026-09-20) is headed "AAMP 3.0 · Draft for public comment" (read 2026-10-23). (as of 2026-10-23). The README of IABTechLab/AAMP says "AAMP is the umbrella initiative under which all of Tech Lab's Agentic work is being developed in partnership with the advertising ecosystem."; OpenProposal "defines how sellers can represent advertising products and respond to buyer briefs in a standardized, agent-readable format", and the release describes AAMP 3.0 as a way "for buyers and sellers to move from an RFP through proposal evaluation and toward a buy commitment". Open proposals in its repositories, all open on 2026-10-23, touch discovery and agent identity: a manifest at `/.well-known/openproposal.json` (OpenProposal issue #29) and IETF Web Bot Auth as the agent identity and request-signing profile (AAMP issue #3); see Open proposals.

**Sources.** [statement] https://github.com/IABTechLab/AAMP (2026-10-23); [statement] https://github.com/IABTechLab/OpenProposal (2026-10-23); [normative] https://github.com/IABTechLab/OpenProposal/blob/v3.0-draft-1/spec/openproposal-3.0.md (2026-10-23); [decision] https://iabtechlab.com/press-releases/iab-tech-lab-introduces-aamp-3-0-with-openproposal/ (2026-10-23) Coverage: see [Evidence](#evidence-iab-tech-lab-aamp).

### Shopify, MCP surfaces for buyer agents, developers and merchants
<a id="shopify"></a>

**Industry, work-item.** Three distinct MCP surfaces are documented or published by Shopify: checkout for buyers' agents (server-side Checkout MCP and, since 2026-09-28, Checkout WebMCP), the local developer package @shopify/dev-mcp (latest version 1.16.0, 2026-09-25), and a remote MCP for merchants at setup.shopify.com/mcp. For the merchant surface the source is Shopify's own toolkit repository; no page on shopify.dev was found for it. A fourth surface is for shoppers' agents: the command-line client @shopify/shop (npm, MIT; version 1.0.3 published 2026-10-08, 01:20 UTC, after 1.0.2 on 2026-10-07), whose repository Shopify/shop has its latest commit d1e8c7c on main (2026-10-08, 01:17 UTC); neither the README, the skill file nor the transactions reference declares a beta or preview status or a list of supported countries (not found, 2026-10-13). Read 2026-11-09: the latest toolkit release is v2.3.0 (2026-10-27, Shopify Flow workflows through the Admin API), after v2.1.2 (2026-10-14) and v2.2.0 (2026-10-16, whose notes say the OpenAI plugin packages "an exact Shopify MCP configuration, and content-free UCP telemetry"); at the tag v2.3.0 the folder skills/ucp still holds SKILL.md and .cursor-plugin/mcp.json still declares the remote MCP at `setup.shopify.com/mcp`. (as of 2026-11-09). Buyers' agents: the documentation says "Use Checkout WebMCP when your agent runs in the buyer's browser. If your agent can run on a server, then use Checkout MCP", and that Checkout WebMCP "implements the UCP checkout capability (dev.ucp.shopping.checkout)"; see ucp and browsers-webmcp. Developers: @shopify/dev-mcp is a local npm package (npm registry, read 2026-10-08 and 2026-10-13, version 1.16.0 published 2026-09-25). Merchants: Shopify-AI-Toolkit v2.1.0 (2026-10-01) states "The Cursor plugin, which Grok Bot also installs from the Cursor Marketplace, now connects to Shopify's remote MCP at `setup.shopify.com/mcp`"; the file .cursor-plugin/mcp.json at that tag declares an http server with that URL and an auth block with a client id, and v2.1.1 (2026-10-08) changes the plugin description to "Create and manage your store with Shopify". The toolkit release v2.0.1 says "Regenerated agent skills from @shopify/shopify-dev-tools 1.17.1"; the package @shopify/shopify-dev-tools returns E404 on the npm registry (2026-10-08 and 2026-10-13). Since v2.0.0 the toolkit publishes UCP as a separate skill: the CHANGELOG entry for 2.0.0 reads "UCP ships separately as `ucp`", and the folder skills/ucp at the tag v2.1.1 (commit 26d0623, 2026-10-08, 00:34 UTC) holds a SKILL.md named ucp whose description begins "Use when the user wants to use the UCP CLI to find, compare, buy, or track products from online merchants" and which requires the binary ucp (read 2026-10-13). Shopper's agents: the README of Shopify/shop (commit d1e8c7c, read 2026-10-13) says the command-line interface (CLI) "lets agents search millions of stores, build carts, check out and pay with the shopper's Shop account, and track orders" and is a client of Shop's MCP server at mcp.shop.com. It says: "Some stores have no agent checkout API (UCP); the agent has to enter a payment credential into the site's checkout form in a browser." For that case the shopper approves a "spend request" on Shop and the agent receives a one-time credential up to the approved limit; the skill file says "Prefer Shop/UCP checkout when available". The reference skills/shop/references/transactions.md says of the agent's report on the purchase "This records your report, not payment confirmation.", of a credential state "`consumed` without an `instrument` does not establish credential delivery or payment.", and of cancelling the request "this does not cancel/refund the purchase or revoke an issued credential." The core of the CLI is a compiled binary (lib/shop.wasm, about 1.9 MB), so its logic is not readable as source; the repository holds nine commits, all of 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-08. No call was made to any MCP or OAuth endpoint; only documentation, repositories and listings were read.

**Author participation.** The author of this map opened issue #62 of Shopify/Shopify-AI-Toolkit on 2026-09-26 (release notes and a source link for @shopify/dev-mcp, and two telemetry questions), still without comments on 2026-10-13 and on 2026-11-09; see Disclosure.

# Part 12: Appendix: Full disclosure

The author has contributed publicly to several of the venues mapped here; those contributions are listed below so readers can weigh them.

Read on 2026-10-13 with GitHub search (issues, pull requests and Discussions authored or commented on by the account `frmoretto`, all dates), a comment-by-comment comparison of that account's comments in the repositories of the venues in this map against this table, and the public archives of the IETF lists DAWN, AUDIT, vcon and web-bot-auth (the latest 40 messages of each) and dns-underscore-keys (3 messages). The working group list of the AAIF Agentic Commerce Working Group (groups.io) shows its messages only with JavaScript or a login, so the entry for it below was not read on the list; it is taken from the message as sent. Not searched: Discord, LFX and lists that are not public. Times are UTC unless stated. The list covers the venues in this map and may be incomplete: contributions that those searches do not return, and other archives, were not searched. Rows dated from 2026-10-14 to 2026-10-23 were added from the reports of the work orders on this map and from the contributions as sent by the author, without repeating the search; the states of PR #41 (vcon) and the reply on PR #966 (UCP) were read with gh on 2026-10-25 and 2026-10-26. Rows dated from 2026-10-23, 07:05 to 2026-11-02 were read with gh on 2026-11-02: comments, reviews, review comments and Discussion posts of the account `frmoretto` since 2026-10-23, 06:21, in the threads named in those rows and in the threads already listed here; a repository-wide search was not repeated. # Part 13: Appendix: Calendar

# Calendar: dated public events in the next 60 days (2026-10-14 to 2026-12-13)

This calendar lists dated public events of the next 60 days declared by the venues; charter milestones beyond 60 days are listed in the entries only, and the individual draft expiries listed here are marked as such: they are not commitments of a venue. Dates declared in public by the venues of `entries/`, read between 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-14. No estimates: a date that a source gives as "approximately" is marked so, and an undated commitment is not listed. Entries are linked by id. Rows already past on 2026-10-13 were removed: the IESG telechat of 2026-10-08 (the agentproto charter, with the WG formed the same day), the TPAC deadline of 2026-10-08 for breakouts scheduled in advance, the TPAC breakout schedule expected on 2026-10-13 (the README now says around 19 October) and the next meeting of the AAIF Security and Privacy group on 2026-10-13.

Events as declared by the venues on 2026-10-14; the rows from 2026-10-22 onwards were checked again on 2026-10-23, and the IETF 127 rows were read on the datatracker on 2026-10-26; check the venue for later changes.
